\mathbb {p} \left(\max  _ {1 \leq i \leq p} \left| \| \boldsymbol {f} _ {i} \| ^ {2} - 1 \right| \geq \frac {(\log p) ^ {2}}{\sqrt {p}}\right) \leq c \exp (- (\log p) ^ {2} / c). \tag {150}
$$

where  $c > 0$  is some constant.

finally, we just need to verify that

$$
\mathbb {p} \left(\max  _ {1 \leq i \leq p} \left| \boldsymbol {f} _ {i} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\xi} \right| \geq \frac {(\log p) ^ {2}}{\sqrt {p}}\right) \leq c \exp \left(- (\log p) ^ {2} / c\right). \tag {151}
$$

for any  $i\in [p]$ , we have  $\pmb{f}_i^\top \pmb {\xi}\sim \mathcal{n}(0,\frac{1}{d})$ . thus, for any  $\varepsilon \geq 0$  the standard gaussian tail bound gives us

$$
\mathbb {p} \left(\left| \boldsymbol {f} _ {i} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {\xi} \right| \geq \varepsilon\right) \leq 2 e ^ {- d \varepsilon^ {2} / 2}.
$$

by setting  $\varepsilon = \frac{(\log p)^2}{\sqrt{p}}$  and applying union bound, we can obtain (151). recall the definition of  $\mathcal{a}_1$  in (22). combining (149), (150) and (151), we complete the proof.

2) concentration of lipschitz functions of gaussian vectors: the results presented in this section are all consequences of the following well-known theorem about the concentration of lipschitz functions of independent gaussian random variables. see e.g., [48, theorem 1.3.4] for a proof.

theorem 3. let  $x \sim \mathcal{n}(\mathbf{0},\mathbf{i}_p)$ . for any  $\kappa$ -lipschitz function  $f(\boldsymbol{x})$  on  $\mathbb{r}^p$  and any  $\varepsilon \geq 0$ ,

$$
\mathbb {p} \left(| f (x) - \mathbb {e} f (x) | \geq \varepsilon\right) \leq 2 \exp \left(- \frac {\varepsilon^ {2}}{4 \kappa^ {2}}\right). \tag {152}
$$

we will also use the integral identity  $\mathbb{e}|x| = \int_0^\infty \mathbb{p}(|x| > t)dt$  to control the moments of concentrated random variables. if a random variable  $x$  satisfies  $\mathbb{p}(|x| > v)\leq ce^{-cv}$  for some  $c,c > 0$ , then for any  $m\in \mathbb{z}^+$ , it holds that

$$
\mathbb {e} | x | ^ {m} \leq c m c ^ {- m} \int_ {0} ^ {\infty} e ^ {- v} v ^ {m - 1} d v = c (m!) c ^ {- m}. \tag {153}
$$

similarly, if  $\mathbb{p}(|x| > v) \leq ce^{-cv^2}$  for some  $c, c > 0$ , then

$$
\mathbb {e} | x | ^ {m} \leq 2 c (m!) c ^ {- \frac {m}{2}}. \tag {154}
$$

in what follows, we will consider probabilistic and moment bounds involving the regressors  $\pmb{a}_t$  and  $\pmb{b}_t$  in (6) and (7), for a fixed feature matrix  $\pmb{f}$ . correspondingly, the notation  $\mathbb{p}_{\backslash f}$  (resp.  $\mathbb{e}_{\backslash f}$ ) refer to the conditional probability (resp. expectation) for a given  $\pmb{f}$ .

lemma 8. let  $\sigma = \mathbb{e}[b_t b_t^\top]$ . there exists  $c > 0$  such that

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\backslash f} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\beta} \right| \geq \varepsilon\right) \leq 2 \exp \left(- \frac {p \varepsilon^ {2}}{c \| \boldsymbol {\beta} \| ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {f} \| ^ {2} \| \sigma^ {\prime} \| _ {\infty} ^ {2}}\right) \tag {155}
$$

and

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\backslash f} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {b} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\beta} \right| \geq \varepsilon\right) \leq 2 \exp \left(- \frac {p \varepsilon^ {2}}{c \| \boldsymbol {\beta} \| ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {\sigma} \|}\right), \tag {156}
$$

for any fixed vector  $\beta \in \mathbb{r}^p$  and  $\varepsilon \geq 0$ . correspondingly, there exists  $c > 0$  such that any  $m \in \mathbb{z}^+$ ,

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {\setminus \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {t}} \boldsymbol {\beta} \right| ^ {m}\right) \leq m! \left(\frac {c \| \boldsymbol {\beta} \| ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {f} \| ^ {2} \| \sigma^ {\prime} \| _ {\infty} ^ {2}}{p}\right) ^ {\frac {m}{2}} \tag {157}
$$

and

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {\setminus \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {b} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\beta} \right| ^ {m}\right) \leq m! \left(\frac {c \| \boldsymbol {\beta} \| ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {\sigma} \|}{p}\right) ^ {\frac {m}{2}}. \tag {158}
$$

proof. as a mapping from  $\mathbb{r}^d$  to  $\mathbb{r}^p$ ,  $\pmb{g} \mapsto \sigma(\pmb{f}^\top \pmb{g})$  is  $(\|\sigma'\|_{\infty}\| \pmb{f}\|)$ -lipschitz continuous. indeed, for any  $\pmb{g}_1, \pmb{g}_2 \in \mathbb{r}^d$ , it is easy to verify that

$$
\left\| \sigma \left(\boldsymbol {f} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {g} _ {1}\right) - \sigma \left(\boldsymbol {f} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {g} _ {2}\right) \right\| ^ {2} \leq \left\| \sigma^ {\prime} \right\| _ {\infty} ^ {2} \left\| \boldsymbol {f} \right\| ^ {2} \left\| \boldsymbol {g} _ {1} - \boldsymbol {g} _ {2} \right\| ^ {2}.
$$

it follows that the function  $f(\pmb{g}) = \frac{1}{\sqrt{p}} \sigma(\pmb{g}^{\top} \pmb{f}) \beta$  is lipschitz continuous. therefore, using (152) we have

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\beta} - \mathbb {e} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\beta}\right) \right| \geq \varepsilon\right) \leq 2 \exp \left(- \frac {p \varepsilon^ {2}}{c \| \boldsymbol {\beta} \| ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {f} \| ^ {2} \| \sigma^ {\prime} \| _ {\infty} ^ {2}}\right). \tag {159}
$$

since  $\sigma (x)$  is an odd function, we have  $\mathbb{e}_{\setminus f}\bigl (\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}} a_t^\top \beta \bigr) = 0$  and thus (155).

to establish (156), we observe that  $\pmb{b}_t$  can be represented as  $\pmb{b}_t = \pmb{\sigma}^{1/2}\widetilde{\pmb{b}}$ , where  $\widetilde{\pmb{b}} \sim \mathcal{n}(\mathbf{0},\pmb{i}_p)$ . it follows that  $\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\pmb{b}_t^\top\pmb{\beta}$  can also be seen as a lipschitz function of a standard normal vector, with a lipschitz constant equal to  $\frac{\|\pmb{\beta}\| \|\pmb{\sigma}^{1/2}\|}{\sqrt{p}}$ . therefore (156) is again a consequence of (152). finally, the moment bounds in (157) and (158) can be obtained by applying (154).

lemma 9. there exists  $c > 0$  such that for any  $t \in [n]$  and  $s \geq \sqrt{\frac{4d}{p}}\| \sigma' \|_{\infty} \| \boldsymbol{f}\|$ ,

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} \| \geq s\right) \leq c \exp \left(- \frac {p s ^ {2}}{c \| \sigma^ {\prime} \| _ {\infty} ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {f} \| ^ {2}}\right). \tag {160}
$$

similarly, for any  $s \geq 2\sqrt{\|\pmb{\sigma}\|}$ , we have

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {b} _ {t} \| \geq s\right) \leq c \exp \left(- \frac {p s ^ {2}}{c \| \boldsymbol {\sigma} \|}\right). \tag {161}
$$

correspondingly, there exists  $c > 0$  such that

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left[ \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} \|\right) ^ {m} \right] \leq \left(\sqrt {\frac {4 d}{p}} \| \sigma^ {\prime} \| _ {\infty} \| \boldsymbol {f} \|\right) ^ {m} + m! \left(\frac {c \| \sigma^ {\prime} \| _ {\infty} \| \boldsymbol {f} \|}{\sqrt {p}}\right) ^ {m}, \tag {162}
$$

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left[ \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {b} _ {t} \|\right) ^ {m} \right] \leq \left(2 \sqrt {\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} \|}\right) ^ {m} + m! \left(c \sqrt {\frac {\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} \|}{p}}\right) ^ {m}, \tag {163}
$$

for any  $t\in [n]$  and  $m\in \mathbb{z}^+$ .

proof. recall that  $\pmb{a}_t = \sigma(\pmb{f}^\top \pmb{g}_t)$  and  $\pmb{g} \mapsto \sigma(\pmb{f}^\top \pmb{g})$  is a  $(\| \sigma' \|_{\infty} \| \pmb{f} \|)$ -lipschitz continuous mapping. it follows that

$\pmb{g} \mapsto \| \sigma (\pmb{f}^{\mathsf{t}}\pmb{g})\| (= \| \pmb{a}\|)$  is a  $(\|\sigma'\|_{\infty}\|\pmb{f}\|)$ -lipschitz continuous function. from (152), there exists  $c > 0$  such that for any  $s > 0$ ,

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} | \| \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} \| - \mathbb {e} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \| \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} \| | > s\right) \leq c \exp \left(- \frac {p s ^ {2}}{c \| \sigma^ {\prime} \| _ {\infty} ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {f} \| ^ {2}}\right). \tag {164}
$$

on the other hand,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} _ {\setminus \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} \|\right) \stackrel {(a)} {=} \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \mathbb {e} _ {\setminus \boldsymbol {f}} \| \sigma (\boldsymbol {f} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {g} _ {t}) - \sigma (\boldsymbol {f} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {0} _ {p}) \| \\ \stackrel {(b)} {\leq} \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {\sigma^ {\prime}} \| _ {\infty} \| \boldsymbol {f} \| \mathbb {e} \| \boldsymbol {g} _ {t} \| \\ \leq \sqrt {\frac {d}{p}} \| \sigma^ {\prime} \| _ {\infty} \| \boldsymbol {f} \|. \tag {165} \\ \end{array}
$$

in step (a), we use the assumption that  $\sigma(\cdot)$  is an odd function and thus  $\sigma(0) = 0$ ; step (b) follows from the lipschitz continuity of the mapping  $\pmb{g} \mapsto \sigma(\pmb{f}^{\mathsf{t}}\pmb{g})$ ; to reach the last inequality, we have used the holder's inequality to get  $\mathbb{e}\|\pmb{g}_t\| \leq \sqrt{\mathbb{e}\|\pmb{g}_t\|^2} = \sqrt{d}$ .

for any  $s \geq \sqrt{\frac{4d}{p}}\| \sigma' \|_{\infty} \| \boldsymbol{f}\|$ , we can use (165) and (164) to deduce that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {p} _ {\setminus f} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} \| \geq s\right) \leq \mathbb {p} _ {\setminus f} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} \| - \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \mathbb {e} \| \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} \| \geq \frac {s}{2}\right) \\ \leq c \exp \left(- \frac {p s ^ {2}}{c \| \sigma^ {\prime} \| _ {\infty} ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {f} \| ^ {2}}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

the proof of (161) is analogous. we write  $\pmb{b}_t = \pmb{\sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{\pmb{b}}_t$  where  $\widetilde{\pmb{b}}_t \sim \mathcal{n}(\mathbf{0}, \pmb{i}_p)$ . therefore, similar to what we did to reach (164), we can show there exists  $c > 0$  such that for any  $s \geq 0$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {p} _ {\setminus \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} | \| \boldsymbol {b} _ {t} \| - \mathbb {e} _ {\setminus \boldsymbol {f}} \| \boldsymbol {b} _ {t} \| | \geq s\right) \\ = \mathbb {p} _ {\backslash f} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \left| \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {\frac {1}{2}} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {b}} _ {t} \right\| - \mathbb {e} _ {\backslash f} \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {\frac {1}{2}} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {b}} _ {t} \right\| \right| \geq s\right) \\ \leq c \exp \left(- \frac {p s ^ {2}}{c \| \boldsymbol {\sigma} \|}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last step follows from the fact that  $\| \sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_t\|$  is a  $\| \sigma^{1 / 2}\|$ -lipschitz function of  $\widetilde{\boldsymbol{b}}_t$ . meanwhile,

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {\mathsf {a}}} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {b} _ {t} \|\right) \leq \sqrt {\frac {1}{p} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {\mathsf {a}}} \| \boldsymbol {\sigma} ^ {\frac {1}{2}} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {b}} _ {t} \| ^ {2}} \leq \sqrt {\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} \|}.
$$

it follows that, for any  $s \geq 2\sqrt{\|\pmb{\sigma}\|}$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {p} _ {\setminus \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {b} _ {t} \| \geq s\right) \leq \mathbb {p} _ {\setminus \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {b} _ {t} \| - \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \mathbb {e} _ {\setminus \boldsymbol {f}} \| \boldsymbol {b} _ {t} \| \geq \frac {s}{2}\right) \\ \leq c \exp \big (- \frac {p s ^ {2}}{c \| \boldsymbol {\sigma} \|} \big). \\ \end{array}
$$

the bounds for the moments  $\mathbb{e}\left[\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\|a_t\|\right]^m$  and  $\mathbb{e}\left[\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\|\boldsymbol{b}_t\|\right]^m$  then directly follow from the probabilistic bounds obtained above and (154).

lemma 10. let  $\mathcal{a}$  be the admissible set of feature matrices defined in (21), and  $h_{\backslash k}$  the leave-one-out hessian matrix

defined in (35). there exists  $c > 0$  such that, for every  $k \in [n]$ ,  $t \neq k$  and  $\varepsilon \geq 0$ ,

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\left| \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {a} _ {k} / p \right| \geq \varepsilon\right) \leq c \exp \left(- (p / c) \min  \left\{\varepsilon^ {2}, \varepsilon \right\}\right), \tag {166}
$$

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\left| \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {b} _ {k} / p \right| \geq \varepsilon\right) \leq c \exp \left(- (p / c) \min  \left\{\varepsilon^ {2}, \varepsilon \right\}\right), \tag {167}
$$

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\left| \boldsymbol {b} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {b} _ {k} / p \right| \geq \varepsilon\right) \leq c \exp \left(- (p / c) \min  \left\{\varepsilon^ {2}, \varepsilon \right\}\right), \tag {168}
$$

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\left| \boldsymbol {b} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {a} _ {k} / p \right| \geq \varepsilon\right) \leq c \exp \left(- (p / c) \min  \left\{\varepsilon^ {2}, \varepsilon \right\}\right). \tag {169}
$$

proof. note that, conditioned on  $\mathbf{f}$ ,  $\mathbf{a}_k$  is independent of  $\mathbf{a}_t^\top \mathbf{h}_{\backslash k}^{-1}$  for  $t \neq k$ . for any  $s \geq \sqrt{\frac{4d}{p}} \| \sigma' \|_{\infty} \| \mathbf{f} \|$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {p} _ {\setminus \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\left| \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {a} _ {k} / p \right| \geq \varepsilon\right) \\ \leq \mathbb {p} _ {\mathbf {f}} \left(\left| \frac {\boldsymbol {a} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1}}{\| \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \|} \frac {\boldsymbol {a} _ {k}}{\sqrt {p}} \right| \geq \frac {\sqrt {p} \varepsilon}{\| \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \|}, \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} \| <   s\right) \\ + \mathbb {p} _ {\mathbf {\psi} \mathbf {f}} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} \| \geq s\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(a)} {\leq} \mathbb {p} _ {\mathbf {f}} \left( \right.\left|\begin{array}{c}\boldsymbol {a} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {t}} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\cdot k} ^ {- 1}\\\| \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {t}} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\cdot k} ^ {- 1} \|\end{array}\right| \frac {\boldsymbol {a} _ {k}}{\sqrt {p}} \left. \right| \geq \frac {\lambda \varepsilon}{2 s}\left. \right) + \mathbb {p} _ {\mathbf {f}} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} \| \geq s\right) \\ \stackrel {(b)} {\leq} c \exp \left(- \frac {p \lambda^ {2} \varepsilon^ {2}}{c s ^ {2} \| \sigma^ {\prime} \| _ {\infty} ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {f} \| ^ {2}}\right) + c \exp \left(- \frac {p s ^ {2}}{c \| \sigma^ {\prime} \| _ {\infty} ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {f} \| ^ {2}}\right), \tag {170} \\ \end{array}
$$

where step (a) follows from the fact that  $h_{\backslash k} \succeq \frac{\lambda}{2} i_p$  for  $f \in \mathcal{a}$  (see remark 2) and hence  $\| h_{\backslash k}^{-1} a_t \| \leq 2\lambda^{-1} \| a_t \|$  and step (b) follows from the concentration inequalities in (155) and (160).

to optimize the bound on the right-hand side of (170), we choose different values of  $s$  according to  $\varepsilon$ . for  $\varepsilon \leq \frac{4d\|\sigma^{\prime}\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\lambda p}\|\boldsymbol{f}\|^{2}$ , we let  $s = \sqrt{\frac{4d}{p}}\|\sigma^{\prime}\|_{\infty}\|\boldsymbol{f}\|$  and get

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\setminus \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\left| \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\setminus k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {a} _ {k} / p \right| \geq \varepsilon\right) \leq 2 c \exp \left(- \frac {p ^ {2} \lambda^ {2} \varepsilon^ {2}}{4 c d \| \sigma^ {\prime} \| _ {\infty} ^ {4} \| \boldsymbol {f} \| ^ {4}}\right).
$$

for  $\varepsilon >\frac{4d\|\sigma^{\prime}\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\lambda p}\| f\|^{2}$ , we let  $s = \sqrt{\lambda\varepsilon}$ , which gives us

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\setminus \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\left| \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\setminus k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {a} _ {k} / p \right| \geq \varepsilon\right) \leq 2 c \exp \left(- \frac {p \lambda \varepsilon}{c \| \sigma^ {\prime} \| _ {\infty} ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {f} \| ^ {2}}\right).
$$

combining these two inequalities and using assumption (a.6) that  $\| \sigma^{\prime}\|_{\infty} < \infty$  and the fact that  $\| f\| \leq 1 + 2\sqrt{\eta}$  for  $f\in \mathcal{a}$ , we get (166). the proofs of (167)-(169) follow exactly the same procedure, and we omit them.

3) the spectral norm of random matrices: we first recall a well-known result on the spectral norm of gaussian random matrices, the proof of which can be found in [47, corollary 7.3.3].

lemma 11. for a random matrix  $\mathbf{f} \in \mathbb{r}^{d \times p}$  with  $f_{ij} \stackrel{i.i.d.}{\sim} \mathcal{n}(0, \frac{1}{d})$ , there exists  $c > 0$  such that for any  $t \geq 0$ ,

$$
\mathbb {p} \left(\| \boldsymbol {f} \| \geq 1 + \sqrt {p / d} + t\right) \leq 2 e ^ {- c d t ^ {2}}. \tag {171}
$$

in particular, choosing  $t = \sqrt{p / d}$  gives us

$$
\mathbb {p} \left(\| \boldsymbol {f} \| \geq 1 + 2 \sqrt {p / d}\right) \leq 2 e ^ {- c p}. \tag {172}
$$

recall the definitions of  $\mathbf{a}_t$  and  $\mathbf{b}_t$  in (6) and (7), respectively. next, we show that the spectral norms of  $\left\| \frac{1}{p} \sum_{t=1}^{n} \mathbf{a}_t \mathbf{a}_t^\top \right\|$  and  $\left\| \frac{1}{p} \sum_{t=1}^{n} \mathbf{b}_t \mathbf{b}_t^\top \right\|$  are bounded with high probability.

lemma 12. there exists some positive constant  $c$  such that, for any fixed  $\pmb{f}$ , the following holds.

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\backslash f} \left(\left\| \frac {1}{p} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {n} \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} ^ {\top} \right\| \geq t\right) \leq 2 \exp \left(- \frac {p t}{4 c \| \sigma^ {\prime} \| _ {\infty} ^ {2} \| f \| ^ {2}}\right) \tag {173}
$$

for any  $t \geq 3c(1 + n/p)\|\pmb{f}\|^2\|\sigma'\|_{\infty}^2$ , and

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\left\| \frac {1}{p} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {n} \boldsymbol {b} _ {t} \boldsymbol {b} _ {t} ^ {\top} \right\| \geq t\right) \leq 2 \exp \left(- \frac {p t}{4 c \| \boldsymbol {\sigma} \|}\right), \tag {174}
$$

for any  $t \geq 3c(1 + n / p)\|\pmb{\sigma}\|$ .

proof. let  $\pmb{x} \in s^{p-1}$  and  $\pmb{u} \in s^{n-1}$  be two fixed vectors with unit norms. for any  $\varepsilon \geq 0$ , we have from (155) that

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\setminus \boldsymbol {f}} \left(| \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} ^ {\intercal} \boldsymbol {x} | \geq \varepsilon\right) \leq 2 e ^ {- \frac {\varepsilon^ {2}}{c \| \sigma^ {\prime} \| _ {\infty} ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {f} \| ^ {2}}},
$$

and thus  $\mathbf{a}_t^\top \mathbf{x}$  is a sub-gaussian random variable. then by the independence of  $\{\mathbf{a}_t\}$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {p} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {a} \boldsymbol {x} \right| \geq \varepsilon\right) = \mathbb {p} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {n} u _ {t} \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {x} \right| \geq \varepsilon\right) \\ \leq 2 e ^ {- \frac {p \varepsilon^ {2}}{c \| \sigma^ {\prime} \| _ {\infty} ^ {2} \| f \| ^ {2}}}, \tag {175} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last step follows from hoeffding's inequality for sub-gaussian random variables [47, theorem 2.6.3].

next, we construct two  $\varepsilon$ -nets:  $\mathcal{n}_p$  on  $s^{p-1}$  and  $\mathcal{n}_n$  on  $s^{n-1}$ , with  $\varepsilon = 1/4$ . it can be shown [47, corollary 4.2.13] that the cardinality of  $\mathcal{n}_p$  and  $\mathcal{n}_n$  satisfies:  $|\mathcal{n}_p| \leq 9^p$  and  $|\mathcal{n}_n| \leq 9^n$ . let  $\mathbf{a}$  be the matrix defined in (9). its operator norm can be bounded as follows [47, lemma 4.4.1]:

$$
\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {a} \| \leq 2 \max  _ {\boldsymbol {x} \in \mathcal {n} _ {p}} \max  _ {\boldsymbol {u} \in \mathcal {n} _ {n}} \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {u} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {a} \boldsymbol {x}. \tag {176}
$$

it follows that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {p} _ {\setminus \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {a} \| \geq \sqrt {t}\right) \leq 2 | \mathcal {n} _ {p} | | \mathcal {n} _ {n} | e ^ {- \frac {p t}{c \| \sigma^ {\prime} \| _ {\infty} ^ {2}} \| \boldsymbol {f} \| ^ {2}} \\ \leq 2 \cdot 9 ^ {n + p} e ^ {- \frac {p t}{c \| \boldsymbol {\sigma^ {\prime}} \| _ {\infty} ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {f} \| ^ {2}}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where to reach the second inequality we have used (175). since  $\| \frac{1}{p}\sum_{t = 1}^{n}\boldsymbol{a}_{t}\boldsymbol{a}_{t}^{\mathsf{t}}\| = \| \frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\boldsymbol {a}\|^{2}$ , the desired inequality in (173) immediately follows if we choose  $t\geq 3c(1 + n / p)\| f\| ^2\| \sigma '\|_\infty^2$ . we omit the proof of (174) as it is completely analogous.

4) concentration of quadratic forms: recall the quadratic form  $\gamma_k(\boldsymbol{r}) = (\boldsymbol{r}^\top \boldsymbol{h}_{\backslash k}^{-1}\boldsymbol{r}) / p$  defined in (43). in what follows, we derive some concentration inequalities for  $\gamma_k(\boldsymbol{r}) = (\boldsymbol{r}^\top \boldsymbol{h}_{\backslash k}^{-1}\boldsymbol{r}) / p$  with  $\boldsymbol{r} = \boldsymbol{a}_k$  or  $\boldsymbol{b}_k$ . we shall use the notation  $\mathbb{p}_k$  (resp.  $\mathbb{e}_k$ ) to denote the conditional probability (resp. expectation) over  $\boldsymbol{a}_k$  and  $\boldsymbol{b}_k$ , with all other random variables, namely,  $\{\boldsymbol{a}_t,\boldsymbol{b}_t\}_{t\neq k}$  and  $\boldsymbol{f}$ , fixed.

lemma 13. there exists  $c > 0$ , such that

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {k} \left(\left| \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {a} _ {k}) - \mathbb {e} _ {k} \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {a} _ {k}) \right| \geq \varepsilon\right) \leq c \exp \left(- (p / c) \min  \left\{\varepsilon^ {2}, \varepsilon \right\}\right) \tag {177}
$$

for every  $\mathbf{f} \in \mathcal{a}$ ,  $k \in [n]$  and  $\varepsilon \geq 0$ . correspondingly, there exists  $c > 0$  such that

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {k} \left[ | \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {a} _ {k}) - \mathbb {e} _ {k} \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {a} _ {k}) | ^ {m} \right] \leq m! (c / p) ^ {m / 2}. \tag {178}
$$

similarly, there exists  $c > 0$  and  $c > 0$ , such that

$$
\left. \mathbb {p} _ {k} \left(\left| \gamma_ {k} \left(\boldsymbol {b} _ {k}\right) - \mathbb {e} _ {k} \gamma_ {k} \left(\boldsymbol {b} _ {k}\right) \right| \geq \varepsilon\right) \leq 2 \exp \left(- c p \min  \left\{\varepsilon^ {2}, \varepsilon \right\}\right) \right. \tag {179}
$$

and

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {k} \left[ \left| \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {b} _ {k}) - \mathbb {e} _ {k} \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {b} _ {k}) \right| ^ {m} \right] \leq m! (c / p) ^ {m / 2} \tag {180}
$$

for every  $\mathbf{f} \in \mathcal{a}$ ,  $k \in [n]$ ,  $\varepsilon \geq 0$ , and  $m \in \mathbb{z}^+$

proof. we first recall the definition of  $h_{\backslash k}$  in (35). since  $h(x)$  is  $\lambda$ -strongly convex, and for  $f \in \mathcal{a}$ ,  $\tau_1 \sigma \preceq \frac{\lambda}{2} i_p$ , we must have  $h_{\backslash k} \succeq \frac{\lambda}{2} i_p$  and thus  $\| h_{\backslash k}^{-1} \| \leq \frac{2}{\lambda}$ . (see remark 2 for additional details.)

the concentration inequality (177) then directly follows from [8, lemma 1] and the fact that  $\|f\| \leq 1 + 2\sqrt{\eta} < \infty$  for  $f \in \mathcal{a}$ . to show (179), we note that  $\pmb{b}_k \sim \mathcal{n}(\pmb{0}, \pmb{\sigma})$ . thus,  $\pmb{b}_k$  can be represented as  $\pmb{b}_k = \pmb{\sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\pmb{z}_k$ , where  $\pmb{z}_k \sim \mathcal{n}(\pmb{0}, \pmb{i}_p)$ . it follows that  $\gamma_k(\pmb{b}_k) = \frac{\pmb{z}_k^\top \pmb{\sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\pmb{h}_{\backslash k}^{-1}\pmb{\sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\pmb{z}_k}{p}$ . since  $\pmb{h}_{\backslash k}^{-1} \preceq \frac{2}{\lambda}\pmb{i}_p$ , we have  $\| \pmb{\sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}}\pmb{h}_{\backslash k}^{-1}\pmb{\sigma}^{\frac{1}{2}} \| \leq \frac{2}{\lambda} \| \pmb{\sigma} \| \leq \frac{2}{\lambda} (\mu_1^2 \| \pmb{f} \| ^2 + \mu_2^2) < \infty$  for  $\pmb{f} \in \mathcal{a}$ . applying the hanson-wright inequality (see, e.g., [47, theorem 6.2.1]) then gives us the concentration inequality in (179).

by applying the inequalities in (153) and (154), we can obtain the moment bounds (178) and (180) from (177) and (179), respectively.

lemma 14. there exists a function  $b(m)$ ,  $m \in \mathbb{z}^+$  such that

$$
\sup  _ {\boldsymbol {f} \in \mathcal {a}, k \in [ n ]} \mathbb {e} _ {k} \left[ \gamma_ {k} ^ {m} \left(\boldsymbol {a} _ {k}\right) \right] \leq b (m) \quad a n d \tag {181}
$$

$$
\sup  _ {\mathbf {k}} \mathbb {e} _ {k} \left[ \gamma_ {k} ^ {m} \left(\boldsymbol {b} _ {k}\right) \right] \leq b (m).
$$

$$
\boldsymbol {f} \in \mathcal {a}, k \in [ n ]
$$

proof. let  $\pmb{r} = \pmb{a}_k$  or  $\pmb{b}_k$ . we first show there exists  $c > 0$  such that

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {k} \left[ \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \right] \leq c, \tag {182}
$$

for any  $k \in [n]$  and  $\pmb{f} \in \mathcal{a}$ . by definition,  $\mathbb{e}_k[\gamma_k(\pmb{r})]$  can be bounded as follows:

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} _ {k} \left[ \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \right] = \frac {1}{p} \operatorname {t r} \left[ \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {e} _ {k} \left(\boldsymbol {r} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\mathrm {t}}\right) \right] \leq \frac {1}{p} \| \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \| _ {\mathrm {f}} \| \mathbb {e} _ {k} \left(\boldsymbol {r} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\mathrm {t}}\right) \| _ {\mathrm {f}} \\ \leq \left\| \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \right\| \cdot \left\| \mathbb {e} _ {k} \left(\boldsymbol {r r} ^ {\top}\right) \right\|. \tag {183} \\ \end{array}
$$

for  $\pmb{r} = \pmb{b}_k$ , recall that  $\mathbb{e}(\pmb{b}_k\pmb{b}_k^\top) = \mu_1^2\pmb{f}^\top \pmb{f} + \mu_2^2\pmb{i}_p$ . moreover, for  $\pmb{f} \in \mathcal{a}$ ,  $\| \pmb{f} \| \leq 1 + 2\sqrt{\eta}$  [see (23)] and  $\| \pmb{h}_{\backslash k}^{-1} \| \leq \frac{2}{\lambda}$ . therefore, from (183), there exists  $c > 0$  such that  $\mathbb{e}_k[\gamma_k(\pmb{b})] \leq c$ , for every  $\pmb{f} \in \mathcal{a}$  and  $k \in [n]$ . for  $\pmb{r} = \pmb{a}_k$ , we can first write  $\| \mathbb{e}(\pmb{a}_k\pmb{a}_k^\top) \|$  as:

$$
\left\| \mathbb {e} \left(\boldsymbol {a} _ {k} \boldsymbol {a} _ {k} ^ {\top}\right) \right\| = \max  _ {\| \boldsymbol {x} \| = 1} \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} \left(\boldsymbol {a} _ {k} \boldsymbol {a} _ {k} ^ {\top}\right) \boldsymbol {x} = \max  _ {\| \boldsymbol {x} \| = 1} \mathbb {e} \left(\boldsymbol {a} _ {k} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {x}\right) ^ {2}. \tag {184}
$$

as is shown in (155), for any  $\boldsymbol{x} \in s^{p-1}$ ,  $\boldsymbol{a}_k^\top \boldsymbol{x}$  is a sub-gaussian variable, with a sub-gaussian norm proportional to  $\| \sigma' \|_{\infty} \| \boldsymbol{f} \|$ . it follows from (154) that

$$
\mathbb {e} \left(\boldsymbol {a} _ {k} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {x}\right) ^ {2} \leq c \| \sigma^ {\prime} \| _ {\infty} ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {f} \| ^ {2} \leq c \| \sigma^ {\prime} \| _ {\infty} ^ {2} (1 + 2 \sqrt {\eta})
$$

for some  $c > 0$ , where the last step is due to (23). substituting this inequality into (184) and (183), we have verified (182) for  $\pmb{r} = \pmb{a}_k$ .

to show (181), we use the following simple inequality due to convexity:  $(x + y)^m \leq 2^{m - 1}(x^m + y^m)$  for  $x, y > 0$  and  $m \in \mathbb{z}^+$ . this allows us to write

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} _ {k} \left[ \gamma_ {k} ^ {m} (\boldsymbol {r}) \right] = \mathbb {e} _ {k} \left| \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) - \mathbb {e} _ {k} \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) + \mathbb {e} _ {k} \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \right| ^ {m} \\ \leq 2 ^ {m - 1} \left(\mathbb {e} _ {k} \left| \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) - \mathbb {e} _ {k} \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \right| ^ {m} + \left| \mathbb {e} _ {k} \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \right| ^ {m}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

applying (178), (180) and (182), we reach the desired bounds in (181).

# f. characterizations of the optimization problems

in this appendix, we collect some useful properties of the optimization problems that we encounter when constructing and analyzing the interpolation path based on lindeberg's method.

for each  $k\in [n]$  , define

$$
r _ {\backslash k} (\boldsymbol {w}) \stackrel {\text {d e f}} {=} \sum_ {t \neq k} \ell \left(\frac {\boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w}}{\sqrt {p}}; y _ {t}\right) + \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {p} h (w _ {j}) + q (\boldsymbol {w}), \tag {185}
$$

where  $q(\boldsymbol{w})$  is the function defined in (28),  $\boldsymbol{r}_t = \boldsymbol{b}_t$  for  $1 \leq t \leq k - 1$ , and  $\boldsymbol{r}_t = \boldsymbol{a}_t$  for  $k + 1 \leq t \leq n$ . let

$$
r _ {k} (\boldsymbol {w}; \boldsymbol {r}) \stackrel {\text {d e f}} {=} r _ {\backslash k} (\boldsymbol {w}) + \ell \left(\frac {\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w}}{\sqrt {p}}; y _ {k}\right), \tag {186}
$$

and

$$
s _ {k} (\boldsymbol {w}; \boldsymbol {r}) \stackrel {\text {d e f}} {=} \phi_ {\backslash k} + \frac {1}{2} \left(\boldsymbol {w} - \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}\right) ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} \left(\boldsymbol {w} - \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}\right) + \ell \left(\frac {\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w}}{\sqrt {p}}; y _ {k}\right), \tag {187}
$$

where  $h_{\backslash k}$  is the hessian matrix defined in (35), and  $\phi_{\backslash k} = \min_{\boldsymbol{w} \in \mathbb{r}^p} r_{\backslash k}(\boldsymbol{w})$ . we will be studying the following three related optimization problems:

$$
\phi_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) = \min  _ {\boldsymbol {w} \in \mathbb {r} ^ {p}} r _ {k} (\boldsymbol {w}; \boldsymbol {r}), \quad \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r}) = \underset {\boldsymbol {w} \in \mathbb {r} ^ {p}} {\arg \min } r _ {k} (\boldsymbol {w}; \boldsymbol {r}), \tag {188}
$$

$$
\phi_ {\backslash k} = \min  _ {\boldsymbol {w} \in \mathbb {r} ^ {p}} r _ {\backslash k} (\boldsymbol {w}), \quad \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} = \underset {\boldsymbol {w} \in \mathbb {r} ^ {p}} {\arg \min } r _ {\backslash k} (\boldsymbol {w}), \tag {189}
$$

$$
\psi_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) = \min  _ {\boldsymbol {w} \in \mathbb {r} ^ {p}} s _ {k} (\boldsymbol {w}; \boldsymbol {r}), \quad \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) = \underset {\boldsymbol {w} \in \mathbb {r} ^ {p}} {\arg \min } s _ {k} (\boldsymbol {w}; \boldsymbol {r}). \tag {190}
$$

as explained in section ii-c, the optimization problems formulated in (188)-(190) can be referred to as the "original problem", the "leave-one-out problem" and the "quadratic approximation problem", respectively.

1) deterministic characterizations: we first show that the quadratic approximation problem (190) allows for convenient closed-form solutions.

lemma 15. for every  $k \in [n]$ , it holds that

$$
\psi_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) = \phi_ {\backslash k} + \mathcal {m} _ {k} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}; \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r})\right), \tag {191}
$$

where  $\mathcal{m}_k(z;\gamma)$  is the moreau envelope of  $\ell (x;y_k)$  as defined in (41), and  $\gamma_{k}(\boldsymbol {r})$  is the quadratic term defined in (43). moreover,

$$
\widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) = \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} - \ell^ {\prime} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}); y _ {k}\right) \frac {\boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r}}{\sqrt {p}} \tag {192}
$$

and

$$
\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) = \operatorname {p r o x} _ {k} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}; \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r})\right), \tag {193}
$$

where  $\operatorname{prox}_k(z;\gamma)$  denotes the proximal operator of  $\ell(x;y_k)$ , i.e.,

$$
\operatorname {p r o x} _ {k} (z; \gamma) \stackrel {{d e f}} {{=}} \underset {x} {\arg \min } \ell (x; y _ {k}) + \frac {(x - z) ^ {2}}{2 \gamma}.
$$

proof. we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \psi_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \\ = \phi_ {\backslash k} + \min  _ {\boldsymbol {w} \in \mathbb {r} ^ {p}} \left\{\frac {1}{2} \left(\boldsymbol {w} - \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}\right) ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} \left(\boldsymbol {w} - \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}\right) \right. \\ \left. + \ell \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w}; y _ {k}\right) \right\} \\ = \phi_ {\backslash k} + \min  _ {\tau} \min  _ {\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\intercal} (\boldsymbol {w} - \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}) = \tau} \left\{\frac {1}{2} (\boldsymbol {w} - \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}) ^ {\intercal} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} (\boldsymbol {w} - \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}) \right. \\ \left. + \ell \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} + \tau ; y _ {k}\right) \right\} \\ = \phi_ {\backslash k} + \min  _ {\tau} \left\{\frac {\tau^ {2}}{2 \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r})} + \ell \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} + \tau ; y _ {k}\right) \right\}. \tag {194} \\ \end{array}
$$

by the definition of moreau envelopes, we immediately get (191). besides, the optimal solution  $\tau^{*}$  of (194) is

$$
\tau^ {*} = \operatorname {p r o x} _ {k} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}; \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r})\right) - \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}. \tag {195}
$$

since  $\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\boldsymbol{r}^{\mathrm{t}}[\widetilde{\boldsymbol{w}}_k(\boldsymbol{r}) - \boldsymbol{w}_{\backslash k}^*] = \tau^*$ , we then get (193). finally, by using the first order optimality condition  $\nabla s_k(\boldsymbol{w};\boldsymbol{r}) = \mathbf{0}$  we can directly get (192).

the next result is a deterministic bound for  $\| \pmb{w}_k^* - \widetilde{\pmb{w}}_k\|$ , i.e., the distance between the true optimal solution and the solution to the quadratic approximation problem.

lemma 16. for any  $\pmb{f} \in \mathcal{a}$  and  $k \in [n]$ , there exists  $c > 0$  such that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r}) - \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \right\| \\ \leq c \left| \ell_ {k} ^ {\prime} \right| ^ {2} l _ {s} \big (\sup  _ {t \neq k} \left\{\left| \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} / p \right| \right\} \left\| \frac {1}{p} \sum_ {t \neq k} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \right\| \cdot \| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} \| \\ \left. + \frac {1}{p} \left[ \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {p} \left(\boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k, i} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {4} \right] ^ {\frac {1}{2}}\right), \tag {196} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\ell_k^\prime \stackrel {\text{def}}{=} \ell^\prime\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}} r^{\mathsf{t}}\widetilde{w}_k(r);y_k\right)$ ,  $h_{\backslash k,i}$  denotes the ith column of  $h_{\backslash k}^{-1}$ , and

$$
l _ {s} \stackrel {\text {d e f}} {=} \sup  _ {t \in [ n ]} \left\{1 + \left| \boldsymbol {g} _ {t} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {\xi} \right| ^ {k _ {1}} \right\}. \tag {197}
$$

here,  $k_{1}\in \mathbb{z}^{+}$  is the constant defined in assumption (a.4).

proof. we follow the proof technique of [29, proposition 3.4]. for notational simplicity, we write  $\boldsymbol{w}^{*} \coloneqq \boldsymbol{w}_{k}^{*}(\boldsymbol{r})$  and  $\widetilde{\boldsymbol{w}} \coloneqq \widetilde{\boldsymbol{w}}_{k}(\boldsymbol{r})$  in the proof. we start by noting that, since  $r_{k}(\boldsymbol{w};\boldsymbol{r})$  is  $\frac{\lambda}{2}$ -strongly convex for  $\boldsymbol{f} \in \mathcal{a}$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \boldsymbol {w} ^ {*} - \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} \| \leq \frac {2}{\lambda} \| \nabla r _ {k} (\boldsymbol {w} ^ {*}; \boldsymbol {r}) - \nabla r _ {k} (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}}; \boldsymbol {r}) \| \\ = \frac {2}{\lambda} \| \nabla r _ {k} (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}}; \boldsymbol {r}) \|, \tag {198} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first inequality is a property of strongly-convex functions (see, e.g., [49, pp. 112-113]), and the last equality is due to the optimality condition  $\nabla r_k(\boldsymbol{w}^*; \boldsymbol{r}) = \mathbf{0}$ . therefore, to prove (196), it suffices to control  $\| \nabla r_k(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{w}}; \boldsymbol{r}) \|$ .

to that end, we note that  $\pmb{w}_{\backslash k}^{*} = \underset {\pmb{w}\in \mathbb{r}^{p}}{\arg \min}r_{\backslash k}(\pmb {w})$  and thus  $\nabla r_{\backslash k}(\pmb{w}_{\backslash k}^{*}) = \mathbf{0}$ . this allows us to write

$$
\begin{array}{l} \nabla r _ {k} (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}}; \boldsymbol {r}) \\ = \nabla r _ {k} (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}}; \boldsymbol {r}) - \nabla r _ {\backslash k} (\boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}) \\ = \sum_ {t \neq k} \ell^ {\prime} \left(\frac {\boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}}}{\sqrt {p}}; y _ {t}\right) \frac {\boldsymbol {r} _ {t}}{\sqrt {p}} + \nabla h (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}}) + \nabla q (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}}) + \ell^ {\prime} \left(\frac {\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}}}{\sqrt {p}}; y _ {k}\right) \frac {\boldsymbol {r}}{\sqrt {p}} \\ - \left[ \sum_ {t \neq k} \ell^ {\prime} \left(\frac {\boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}}{\sqrt {p}}; y _ {t}\right) \frac {\boldsymbol {r} _ {t}}{\sqrt {p}} + \nabla h (\boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}) + \nabla q (\boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}) \right] \\ = \left[ \frac {1}{p} \sum_ {t \neq k} \ell^ {\prime \prime} \left(v _ {t}; y _ {t}\right) \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\mathsf {t}} + \nabla^ {2} q (\boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}) \right] (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} - \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}) \\ + \ell^ {\prime} \left(\frac {\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}}}{\sqrt {p}}; y _ {k}\right) \frac {\boldsymbol {r}}{\sqrt {p}} + \nabla h (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}}) - \nabla h \left(\boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*}\right), \tag {199} \\ \end{array}
$$

where in reaching the last step we have used the intermediate value theorem, with  $v_{t}$  being some number that lies between  $\frac{\boldsymbol{r}_t^\top \boldsymbol{w}_{\backslash k}^*}{\sqrt{p}}$  and  $\frac{\boldsymbol{r}_t^\top \widetilde{\boldsymbol{w}}}{\sqrt{p}}$ . from (192) and the definition of  $h_{\backslash k}$  in (35), we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left[ \frac {1}{p} \sum_ {t \neq k} \ell^ {\prime \prime} \left(\frac {\boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}}{\sqrt {p}}; y _ {t}\right) \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \right. \\ \left. + \operatorname {d i a g} \left\{h ^ {\prime \prime} \left(w _ {\backslash k, i} ^ {*}\right) \right\} + \nabla^ {2} q \left(\boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}\right) \right] \times \left(\widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} - \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}\right) \\ + \ell^ {\prime} \left(\frac {\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}}}{\sqrt {p}}; y _ {k}\right) \frac {\boldsymbol {r}}{\sqrt {p}} = \mathbf {0}. \tag {200} \\ \end{array}
$$

substituting this inequality into (199) then gives us

$$
\begin{array}{l} \nabla r _ {k} (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}}; \boldsymbol {r}) \\ = \frac {1}{p} \sum_ {t \neq k} \left[ \ell^ {\prime \prime} \left(v _ {t}; y _ {t}\right) - \ell^ {\prime \prime} \left(\frac {\boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}}{\sqrt {p}}; y _ {t}\right) \right] \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} - \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}) \\ + \nabla h (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}}) - \nabla h \left(\boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}\right) - \operatorname {d i a g} \left\{h ^ {\prime \prime} \left(w _ {\backslash k, i} ^ {*}\right) \right\} \left(\widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} - \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}\right) \\ = - \frac {1}{p} \sum_ {t \neq k} \left[ \ell^ {\prime \prime} \left(v _ {t}; y _ {t}\right) - \ell^ {\prime \prime} \left(\frac {\boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}}{\sqrt {p}}; y _ {t}\right) \right] \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \left(\frac {\ell_ {k} ^ {\prime} h _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r}}{\sqrt {p}}\right) \\ + \nabla h (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}}) - \nabla h \left(\boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}\right) - \operatorname {d i a g} \left\{h ^ {\prime \prime} \left(w _ {\backslash k, i} ^ {*}\right) \right\} \left(\widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} - \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}\right), \tag {201} \\ \end{array}
$$

where in the last step,  $\ell_k' = \ell' \left( \frac{r^\top \widetilde{w}}{\sqrt{p}}; y_k \right)$ , and we have used (192). by the intermediate value theorem,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \frac {1}{p} \sum_ {t \neq k} \left[ \ell^ {\prime \prime} \left(v _ {t}; y _ {t}\right) - \ell^ {\prime \prime} \left(\frac {\boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}}{\sqrt {p}}; y _ {t}\right) \right] \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \right\| \\ \leq \sup  _ {t \neq k} \left\{\left| \ell^ {\prime \prime \prime} \left(u _ {t}; y _ {t}\right) \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \left(\widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} - \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}\right) \right| \right\} \left\| \frac {1}{p} \sum_ {t \neq k} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \right\| \\ \leq c \underbrace {\sup  _ {t \in [ n ]} \left\{1 + \left| \boldsymbol {g} _ {t} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {\xi} \right| ^ {k _ {1}} \right\}} _ {= l _ {s}} \sup  _ {t \neq k} \left\{\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \left(\widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} - \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}\right) \right| \right\} \left\| \frac {1}{p} \sum_ {t \neq k} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \right\|, \tag {202} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $u_{t}$  is some number lying between  $v_{t}$  and  $\frac{\boldsymbol{r}_t^\top\boldsymbol{w}_{\backslash k}^*}{\sqrt{p}}$ , and the last step follows from assumption (a.4). from (201) and (202), there exists  $c > 0$

$$
\| \nabla r _ {k} (\tilde {\boldsymbol {w}}; \boldsymbol {r}) \|
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq c l _ {s} \left| \ell_ {k} ^ {\prime} \right| \sup  _ {t \neq k} \left\{\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\mathsf {t}} (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} - \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}) \right| \right\} \left\| \frac {1}{p} \sum_ {t \neq k} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \right\| \cdot \left\| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} \right\| \\ + \frac {\| h ^ {\prime \prime \prime} \| _ {\infty}}{2} \big [ \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {p} (\widetilde {w} _ {i} - w _ {\backslash k, i} ^ {*}) ^ {4} \big ] ^ {1 / 2} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} = c l _ {s} \left| \ell_ {k} ^ {\prime} \right| ^ {2} \sup  _ {t \neq k} \left\{\left| \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} / p \right| \right\} \left\| \frac {1}{p} \sum_ {t \neq k} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \right\| \cdot \left\| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} \right\| \\ + \frac {c \left| \ell_ {k} ^ {\prime} \right| ^ {2}}{p} \left[ \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {p} \left(\boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k, i} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {4} \right] ^ {1 / 2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where in the last step, we have used (192) and the assumption that  $\| h^{\prime \prime \prime}\|_{\infty} < \infty$ . substituting this inequality into (198) and using the fact that  $l_{s}\geq 1$ , we conclude the proof.

2) bounding  $\| \mathbf{w}_{\mathbf{k}}^{*}\|$ : we will introduce a function  $g(\boldsymbol{w})$  to wrap up all the terms in (186), except the loss function, i.e.,

$$
g (\boldsymbol {w}) = \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {p} h \left(w _ {j}\right) + q (\boldsymbol {w}), \tag {203}
$$

where  $q(\pmb{w})$  is defined in (28).

lemma 17. let  $\boldsymbol{w}_k^*(\boldsymbol{r})$  denote either  $\boldsymbol{w}_k^*(\boldsymbol{a}_k)$  or  $\boldsymbol{w}_k^*(\boldsymbol{b}_k)$ , and  $\boldsymbol{w}_{\backslash k}^*$  be the leave-one-out solution in (189). there exists  $c, c > 0$  such that for every  $k \in [n]$ ,

$$
\mathbb {p} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r}) \| \geq c\right) \leq c e ^ {- (\log p) ^ {2} / c} \tag {204}
$$

and

$$
\mathbb {p} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} \| \geq c\right) \leq c e ^ {- (\log p) ^ {2} / c}. \tag {205}
$$

proof. recall the definition of the set  $\mathcal{a}_2$  in (23). we start by noting that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {p} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r}) \| \geq c\right) \\ \leq \mathbb {p} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r}) \| \geq c \cap \boldsymbol {f} \in \mathcal {a} _ {2}\right) + \mathbb {p} (\boldsymbol {f} \in \mathcal {a} _ {2} ^ {c}) \\ \leq \sup  _ {\boldsymbol {f} \in \mathcal {a} _ {2}} \mathbb {p} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r}) \| \geq c\right) + 2 e ^ {- c p}, \tag {206} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last inequality is due to (25). therefore, to show (204), it suffices to bound the conditional probability  $\mathbb{p}_{\setminus f}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\|\boldsymbol{w}_k^*(\boldsymbol{r})\| \geq c\right)$  for any fixed  $\boldsymbol{f} \in \mathcal{a}_2$ .

on the one hand, since  $\ell (x;y)\geq 0$  , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} g \left(\boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r})\right) \leq \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {n} \ell \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r}); y _ {t}\right) + g \left(\boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r})\right) \\ \leq \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {n} \ell (0; y _ {t}) + g (\mathbf {0}), \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last step is due to the fact that  $\pmb{w}_k^*(\pmb{r})$  is the optimal solution. on the other hand, for  $\pmb{f} \in \mathcal{a}_2$ ,  $g(\pmb{w})$  is  $\frac{\lambda}{2}$ -strongly convex. this then gives us

$$
\begin{array}{l} g \left(\boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r})\right) \geq g (\mathbf {0}) + \nabla^ {\intercal} g (\mathbf {0}) \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r}) + \frac {\lambda}{4} \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r}) \| ^ {2} \\ \geq g (\mathbf {0}) - \| \nabla g (\mathbf {0}) \| \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r}) \| + \frac {\lambda}{4} \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r}) \| ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

combining the above upper and lower bounds for  $g(\boldsymbol{w}_k^*)$ , we have

$$
\frac {\lambda}{4} \left\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r}) \right\| ^ {2} - \left\| \nabla g (\boldsymbol {0}) \right\| \left\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r}) \right\| \leq \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {n} \ell (0; y _ {t})
$$

and thus

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {\left\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r}) \right\|}{\sqrt {p}} \leq \frac {2 \| \nabla g (\boldsymbol {0}) \| + 2 \sqrt {\left\| \nabla g (\boldsymbol {0}) \right\| ^ {2} + \lambda \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {n} \ell (0 ; y _ {t})}}{\lambda \sqrt {p}} \\ \leq \frac {2}{\lambda \sqrt {p}} \left[ 2 \| \nabla g (\mathbf {0}) \| + \left(\lambda \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {n} \ell \left(0; y _ {t}\right)\right) ^ {1 / 2} \right]. \tag {207} \\ \end{array}
$$

by its definition in (203),  $\nabla g(\mathbf{0}) = h'(0)\mathbf{1}_p + \tau_2\mu_1\sqrt{p}\mathbf{f}^\top \boldsymbol{\xi}$  and thus

$$
\| \nabla g (\mathbf {0}) \| \leq c _ {1} \sqrt {p},
$$

where  $c_1 = |h'(0)| + \tau_2\mu_1(1 + 2\sqrt{\eta})$  and we have used (23). it then follows from (207) that

$$
\frac {\left\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r}) \right\|}{\sqrt {p}} \leq \frac {2}{\lambda} \left[ 2 c _ {1} + \left(\frac {\lambda}{p} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {n} \ell (0; y _ {t})\right) ^ {1 / 2} \right]. \tag {208}
$$

from assumption (a.4), we know there exists some  $c_2, c_2' > 0$  such that for any  $b > 0$

$$
\mathbb {p} \left(\frac {1}{p} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {n} \ell (0; y _ {t}) \geq c _ {2}\right) \tag {209}
$$

$$
\leq \mathbb {p} \left(\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {n} \left| s _ {t} \right| ^ {k _ {1}} \geq 2 c _ {2} ^ {\prime}\right)
$$

$$
\leq \mathbb {p} \left(\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {n} | s _ {t} | ^ {k _ {1}} \mathbb {1} _ {\{| s _ {t} | \leq b \}} \geq 2 c _ {2} ^ {\prime}\right) + \mathbb {p} \left(\max  _ {t \in [ n ]} | s _ {t} | > b\right)
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(a)} {\leq} \mathbb {p} \left(\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {n} \left(\left| s _ {t} \right| ^ {k _ {1}} \mathbb {1} _ {\left\{\left| s _ {t} \right| \leq b \right\}} - e _ {b}\right) \geq c _ {2} ^ {\prime}\right) \\ + \mathbb {p} \left(\max  _ {t \in [ n ]} | s _ {t} | > b\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\stackrel {(b)} {\leq} \exp \left(- \frac {2 n \left(c _ {2} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {2}}{b ^ {2 k _ {1}}}\right) + 2 n \exp \left(- b ^ {2} / 2\right), \tag {210}
$$

where  $s_t \stackrel{i.i.d.}{\sim} \mathcal{n}(0,1)$ ,  $e_b \coloneqq \mathbb{e}\big(|s_t|^{k_1} \mathbb{1}_{\{|s_t| \leq b\}}\big)$ , (a) follows from the fact  $e_b \leq \mathbb{e}|s_t|^{k_1} < \infty$  and (b) follows from hoeffding's inequality for bounded random variables [47, theorem 2.2.6] and the tail bound for standard gaussian:  $\mathbb{p}(|s| \geq t) \leq 2e^{-t^2 / 2}$ . letting  $b = 1 + \log p$  in (210), we have

$$
\mathbb {p} \left(\frac {1}{p} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {n} \ell (0; y _ {t}) \geq c _ {2}\right) \leq c _ {3} e ^ {- (\log p) ^ {2} / c _ {3}}, \tag {211}
$$

for some  $c_3 > 0$ . combining (208) and (211) and choosing  $c_4 = (2 / \lambda)(2c_1 + \sqrt{\lambda}\sqrt{c_2})$ , we get

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\setminus \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r}) \| \geq c _ {4}\right) \leq c _ {3} e ^ {- (\log p) ^ {2} / c _ {3}}.
$$

as this holds uniformly over all  $\mathcal{f} \in \mathcal{a}_2$ , we get (204) from (206). the proof of (205) follows exactly the same steps, and we omit it.

lemma 18. let  $\pmb{w}_{k}^{*}(\pmb{r})$  denote either  $\pmb{w}_{k}^{*}(\pmb{a}_{k})$  or  $\pmb{w}_{k}^{*}(\pmb{b}_{k})$ , and  $\pmb{w}_{\backslash k}^{*}$  be the leave-one-out solution in (189). there exists a function  $b(m)$  of  $m \in \mathbb{z}^+$  such that for any  $\pmb{f} \in \mathcal{a}_2$ ,  $p \geq 2$  and  $k \in [n]$ ,

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r}) \|\right) ^ {m} \leq b (m) (\log p) ^ {m k _ {1} / 2} \tag {212}
$$

and

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} \|\right) ^ {m} \leq b (m) (\log p) ^ {m k _ {1} / 2}, \tag {213}
$$

where  $k_{1}$  is the constant defined in assumption (a.4).

proof. using the simple inequality  $\sqrt{x} < 1 + x$  for  $x \geq 0$ , we can deduce from (208) that

$$
\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \left\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r}) \right\| \leq c \left[ 1 + \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {n} \ell (0; y _ {t}) \right].
$$

it follows that

$$
\left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \left\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r}) \right\|\right) ^ {m} \leq (2 c) ^ {m} \left[ 1 + \left(\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {n} \ell \left(0; y _ {t}\right)\right) ^ {m} \right]. \tag {214}
$$

according to assumption (a.4), there exists  $c > 0$  such that for any  $\varepsilon \geq 0$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {p} \left[ \left(\frac {1}{n} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {n} \ell (0; y _ {t})\right) ^ {m} \geq \varepsilon \right] \leq n \mathbb {p} \left(\ell (0; y _ {t}) \geq \varepsilon^ {1 / m}\right) \\ \leq c p \exp \left(- \varepsilon^ {\frac {2}{m k _ {1}}} / c\right). \tag {215} \\ \end{array}
$$

then by the integral identity  $\mathbb{e}|x| = \int_0^\infty \mathbb{p}(|x| > t)dt$ , there exists some  $c > 0$  such that for  $p \geq 2$ ,

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ \frac {1}{n} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {n} \ell (0; y _ {t}) \right] ^ {m} \leq m! \left(c \log p\right) ^ {m k _ {1} / 2}. \tag {216}
$$

combining (214) and (216) gives us (212). the proof of (213) follows the same steps, and we omit it.

3) bounding  $|\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}} \mathbf{r}^{\mathsf{t}} \widetilde{\mathbf{w}}_k(\mathbf{r})|$ :

lemma 19. let  $\widetilde{\boldsymbol{w}}_k(\boldsymbol{r})$  be the optimal solution to the quadratic optimization problem as defined in (190). there exists  $c > 0$  such that for any  $k\in [n]$  and  $\varepsilon \geq 0$

$$
\mathbb {p} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \right| \geq \varepsilon\right) \leq c \exp \left(- \varepsilon^ {\frac {4}{k _ {1} + 2}} / c\right) + c e ^ {- (\log p) ^ {2} / c}, \tag {217}
$$

where  $\pmb{r} = \pmb{a}_k$  or  $\pmb{b}_k$  and  $k_{1}\in \mathbb{z}^{+}$  is the constant defined in assumption (a.4).

proof. we first show there exists  $c > 0$  such that for any  $\varepsilon \geq 0$ ,

$$
\mathbb {p} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} \right| \geq \varepsilon\right) \leq c e ^ {- \varepsilon^ {2} / c} + c e ^ {- (\log p) ^ {2} / c}, \tag {218}
$$

where  $\pmb{w}_{\backslash k}^{*}$  is the leave-one-out solution defined in (189).

note that  $\pmb{w}_{\backslash k}^{*}$  is independent of  $a_{k}$ . from (155), there exists  $c > 0$  such that for any  $\varepsilon \geq 0$ , when conditioned on  $\pmb{f}$  and  $\pmb{w}_{\backslash k}^{*}$ ,

$$
\mathbb {p} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {a} _ {k} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} \right| \geq \varepsilon \mid \boldsymbol {f}, \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}\right) \leq c e ^ {- \frac {p \varepsilon^ {2}}{c \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {f} \| ^ {2} \| \sigma^ {\prime} \| _ {\infty} ^ {2}}}. \tag {219}
$$

define the following event:

$$
\mathcal {e} \stackrel {\text {d e f}} {=} \left\{\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} \| \leq c, \| \boldsymbol {f} \| \leq 1 + 2 \sqrt {\eta} \right\},
$$

where  $c$  is the same constant as the one in (205). then it holds that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {p} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {a} _ {k} ^ {\textsf {t}} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} \right| \geq \varepsilon\right) \\ = \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {f}, \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}} \mathbb {p} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {a} _ {k} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} \right| \geq \varepsilon \mid \boldsymbol {f}, \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}\right) \\ \leq \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {f}, \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {\mathcal {e}} \mathbb {p} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {a} _ {k} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} \right| \geq \varepsilon \mid \boldsymbol {f}, \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}\right) \right] + \mathbb {p} (\mathcal {e} ^ {c}). \\ \end{array}
$$

it then follows from (171), (205), (219) and assumption (a.6) that there exists  $c > 0$  such that

$$
\mathbb {p} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {a} _ {k} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} \right| \geq \varepsilon\right) \leq c e ^ {- \varepsilon^ {2} / c} + c e ^ {- (\log p) ^ {2} / c}, \tag {220}
$$

for every  $k \in [n]$  and  $\varepsilon \geq 0$ . the case of  $\boldsymbol{r} = \boldsymbol{b}_k$  for (218) can be proved in the same way and we omit its proof.

next, we show (217) by using the characterization in (193). since

$$
\operatorname {p r o x} _ {k} (0; \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r})) = \arg \min  _ {x} \left\{\frac {x ^ {2}}{2} + \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \ell (x; y _ {k}) \right\} \tag {221}
$$

and  $\gamma_k(r), \ell(x; y_k) \geq 0$ , we can get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {1}{2} \operatorname {p r o x} _ {k} (0; \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r})) ^ {2} \\ \leq \frac {1}{2} \operatorname {p r o x} _ {k} (0; \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r})) ^ {2} + \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \ell \left(\operatorname {p r o x} _ {k} (0; \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r})); y _ {k}\right) \\ \leq \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \ell (0; y _ {k}), \\ \end{array}
$$

where in the last step, we substitute  $x = 0$  in the right-hand side of (221) and use the optimality of  $\operatorname{prox}_k(0; \gamma_k(\boldsymbol{r}))$ . this gives us  $|\operatorname{prox}_k(0; \gamma_k(\boldsymbol{r}))| \leq \sqrt{2\gamma_k(\boldsymbol{r}) \ell(0; y_k)}$ . by the non-expansiveness of proximal operators, we can get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \right| = \left| \operatorname {p r o x} _ {k} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}; \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r})\right) \right| \\ \leq \sqrt {2 \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \ell (0 ; y _ {k})} + \left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} \right|. \tag {222} \\ \end{array}
$$

from assumption (a.4),  $\ell(0; y_k) \leq c (1 + |g_k^\mathsf{t} \pmb{\xi}|^{k_1})$ , with  $g_k^\mathsf{t} \pmb{\xi} \sim \mathcal{n}(0, 1)$ , so by standard gaussian concentration bound, there exists  $c > 0$  such that for any  $\varepsilon \geq 0$ ,

$$
\mathbb {p} \left(\ell (0; y _ {k}) \geq \varepsilon\right) \leq c \exp \left(- \varepsilon^ {2 / k _ {1}} / c\right). \tag {223}
$$

on the other hand, from lemma 13 and lemma 14, there exists  $c > 0$  such that for any  $\varepsilon \geq 0$

$$
\mathbb {p} \left(\gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \geq \varepsilon\right) \leq c \exp (- \varepsilon / c). \tag {224}
$$

then it follows from (222), (223), (224) and (218) that there exists  $c > 0$  such that for any  $\varepsilon \geq 0$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {p} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \right| \geq \varepsilon\right) \\ \leq \mathbb {p} \left[ \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \geq \left(\frac {\varepsilon}{2 \sqrt {2}}\right) ^ {\frac {2 k _ {1}}{k _ {1} + 2}} \right] + \mathbb {p} \left[ \ell (0, y _ {k}) \geq \left(\frac {\varepsilon}{2 \sqrt {2}}\right) ^ {\frac {4}{k _ {1} + 2}} \right] \\ + \mathbb {p} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} \right| \geq \varepsilon / 2\right) \\ \leq c \exp \left(- \varepsilon^ {\frac {4}{k _ {1} + 2}} / c\right) + c e ^ {- (\log p) ^ {2} / c}. \tag {225} \\ \end{array}
$$

this concludes our proof.

![](images/1d3b58600a39dd218505a3b1b2724544305255ec144097bdbc3db036bcc8812e.jpg)

lemma 20. there exists a function  $b(m)$ ,  $m \in \mathbb{z}^+$ , such that for every  $\pmb{f} \in \mathcal{a}$  and  $p \geq 2$ ,

$$
\left. \mathbb {e} _ {\backslash f} \left| \ell^ {\prime} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}); y _ {k}\right) \right| ^ {m} \leq b (m) (\log p) ^ {m k _ {1}}, \right. \tag {226}
$$

where  $\pmb{r} = \pmb{a}_k$  or  $\pmb{b}_k$  and  $k_{1}\in \mathbb{z}^{+}$  is the constant defined in assumption (a.4).

proof. we start by showing that  $\mathbb{e}_{\backslash f}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}} r^{\top}w_{\backslash k}^{*}\right|^{m}$  is bounded. indeed, from the independence of  $w_{\backslash k}^{*}$  and  $r$ , we can apply (157), (158), and (213) to get for  $p \geq 2$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} \right| ^ {m} \leq b _ {1} (m) \mathbb {e} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} \|\right) ^ {m} \tag {227} \\ \leq b _ {2} (m) (\log p) ^ {m k _ {1} / 2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $b_{1}(m)$  and  $b_{2}(m)$  are two constants that depend on  $m$ . using (222), we have for  $p \geq 2$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} _ {\setminus \boldsymbol {f}} \left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \right| ^ {m} \\ \leq \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {\mathcal {f}}} \left(\sqrt {2 \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \ell (0 ; y _ {k})} + \left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} \right|\right) ^ {m} \\ \leq 3 ^ {m - 1} \mathbb {e} _ {\backslash f} \left(\left[ \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \right] ^ {m} + \left[ \ell (0; y _ {k}) \right] ^ {m} + \left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} \right| ^ {m}\right) \\ \leq b _ {3} (m) (\log p) ^ {m k _ {1} / 2}, \tag {228} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $b_{3}(m)$  is a constant that depend on  $m$  and the last step follows from (181), (227) and assumption (a.4).

now we are ready to obtain (226). by assumption (a.4), there exists  $c, c_1 > 0$  such that for any  $x$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \ell^ {\prime} (x; y _ {k}) \right| \\ \leq \left| \ell^ {\prime} (0; y _ {k}) \right| + \int_ {- | x |} ^ {| x |} \left| \ell^ {\prime \prime} (t; y _ {k}) \right| d t \\ \leq \left| \ell^ {\prime} \left(0; y _ {k}\right) \right| + 2 \left| \ell^ {\prime \prime} \left(0; y _ {k}\right) \right| | x | + \int_ {- | x |} ^ {| x |} \int_ {- | t |} ^ {| t |} \left| \ell^ {\prime \prime \prime} \left(u; y _ {k}\right) \right| d u d t \\ \leq c \left(\left| \boldsymbol {g} _ {k} ^ {\intercal} \boldsymbol {\xi} \right| ^ {k _ {1}} + 1\right) \left(1 + 2 | x | + 2 | x | ^ {2}\right) \\ \leq c _ {1} \left(\left| x \right| ^ {2} + 1\right) \left(\left| \boldsymbol {g} _ {k} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {\xi} \right| ^ {k _ {1}} + 1\right), \tag {229} \\ \end{array}
$$

so we can get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} _ {\setminus \boldsymbol {f}} \left| \ell^ {\prime} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}); y _ {k}\right) \right| ^ {m} \\ \leq c _ {1} (m) \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {\mathcal {f}}} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \right| ^ {2} + 1\right) ^ {m} \left(| \boldsymbol {g} _ {k} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\xi} | ^ {k _ {1}} + 1\right) ^ {m} \\ \leq c _ {2} (m) \sqrt {\mathbb {e} _ {\setminus f} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \right| ^ {4 m} + 1\right)} \sqrt {\mathbb {e} \left(| \boldsymbol {g} _ {k} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\xi} | ^ {2 m k _ {1}} + 1\right)}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $c_1(m), c_2(m) > 0$  are two constants that depend on  $m$ . then (226) can be proved by using (228) and standard moment bounds for  $\pmb{g}_k^\top \pmb{\xi} \sim \mathcal{n}(0,1)$ .

4) bounding  $\| \mathbf{w}_k^* (\mathbf{r}) - \tilde{\mathbf{w}}_k(\mathbf{r})\|$ :

lemma 21. there exists  $c > 0$  such that for every  $k \in [n]$  and  $\varepsilon \geq 0$ ,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {p} \left(\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r}) - \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \| \geq \varepsilon\right) \\ \leq c p \exp \left[ - \min  \left\{\left(\sqrt {p} \varepsilon\right) ^ {\frac {2}{5 k _ {1} + 6}}, \left(\sqrt {p} \varepsilon\right) ^ {\frac {4}{5 k _ {1} + 6}}, (\log p) ^ {2} \right\} / c \right], \tag {230} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\mathbf{r} = \mathbf{a}_k$  or  $\mathbf{b}_k$  and  $k_{1}\in \mathbb{z}^{+}$  is the constant defined in assumptions (a.4).

proof. for notational simplicity, we write  $\boldsymbol{w}^{*} \coloneqq \boldsymbol{w}_{k}^{*}(\boldsymbol{r})$  and  $\widetilde{\boldsymbol{w}} \coloneqq \widetilde{\boldsymbol{w}}_{k}(\boldsymbol{r})$  in the proof.  $c > 0$  and  $c > 0$  denote constants whose values can change from one line to the other. from (196), we have that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \boldsymbol {w} ^ {*} - \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} \| \\ \leq c \left| \ell_ {k} ^ {\prime} \right| ^ {2} l _ {s} \left(\sup  _ {t \neq k} \left\{\left| \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} / p \right| \right\} \left\| \frac {1}{p} \sum_ {t \neq k} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \right\| \cdot \| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} \| \right. \\ \left. + \frac {1}{p} \left[ \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {p} \left(\boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k, i} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {4} \right] ^ {\frac {1}{2}}\right), \tag {231} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\pmb{r} = \pmb{a}_k$  or  $\pmb{b}_k$  and  $h_{\backslash k,i}$  denotes the  $i$ th column of  $\pmb{h}_{\backslash k}^{-1}$ . therefore, to show (230), it suffices to control each term on the right-hand side of (231).

(i)  $\frac{1}{p}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{p}(\boldsymbol{h}_{\backslash k,i}^{\mathsf{t}}\boldsymbol{r})^{4}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}$ . conditioned on  $\boldsymbol{f} \in \mathcal{a}_2$ ,  $\|\boldsymbol{h}_{\backslash k}^{-1}\| \leq \frac{2}{\lambda}$  and hence  $\|\boldsymbol{h}_{\backslash k,i}\| \leq \frac{2}{\lambda}$ , for any  $i \in [p]$ . applying (155) and (156) and taking into account the independence between  $\boldsymbol{h}_{\backslash k,i}$  and  $\boldsymbol{r}$ , we can find a constant  $c > 0$  such that for any  $\varepsilon \geq 0$ ,  $i \in [p]$  and  $\boldsymbol{f} \in \mathcal{a}_2$ ,

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\left| \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k, i} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r} \right| \geq \varepsilon\right) \leq c e ^ {- \varepsilon^ {2} / c}. \tag {232}
$$

by (172),  $\mathbb{p}(\mathcal{a}_2) \geq 1 - ce^{-p/c}$ . it then follows that

$$
\mathbb {p} \left(\left| \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k, i} ^ {\intercal} \boldsymbol {r} \right| \geq \varepsilon\right) \leq c e ^ {- \varepsilon^ {2} / c} + c e ^ {- p / c}.
$$

applying the union bound then gives us

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {p} \left(\frac {1}{p} \left[ \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {p} \left(\boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k, i} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {4} \right] ^ {\frac {1}{2}} \geq \varepsilon\right) \leq \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {p} \mathbb {p} \left(| \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k, i} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r} | > p ^ {\frac {1}{4}} \varepsilon^ {\frac {1}{2}}\right) \\ \leq c p e ^ {- \sqrt {p} \varepsilon / c} + c p e ^ {- p / c}. \tag {233} \\ \end{array}
$$

(ii)  $|\ell_k'|^2 l_s$ . recall from (196) that  $\ell_k' = \ell'(\frac{r^\top \widetilde{w}}{\sqrt{p}}; y_k)$  and  $l_s \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \sup_{t \in [n]} \{1 + |g_t^\top \xi|^{k_1}\}$ . from (229), we know there exists  $c > 0$  such that for any  $x$ ,  $|\ell'(x; y_k)|^2 \leq c(|x|^4 + 1) \left( |g_k^\top \xi|^{2k_1} + 1 \right)$  and thus

$$
\left| \ell^ {\prime} \left(x; y _ {k}\right) \right| ^ {2} l _ {s} \leq c _ {1} \left(\left| x \right| ^ {4} + 1\right) \left(\sup  _ {t \in [ n ]} \left| \boldsymbol {g} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\xi} \right| ^ {3 k _ {1}} + 1\right), \tag {234}
$$

for some  $c_1 > 0$ . therefore, there exists  $c > 0$  such that for any sufficiently large  $\varepsilon > 0$ ,

$$
\mathbb {p} \left(\left| \ell_ {k} ^ {\prime} \right| ^ {2} l _ {s} \geq c _ {1} \varepsilon\right)
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq \mathbb {p} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} \right| ^ {4} + 1 \geq 2 \left(\frac {\varepsilon}{4}\right) ^ {\frac {2 k _ {1} + 4}{5 k _ {1} + 4}}\right) \\ + \mathbb {p} \left(\sup  _ {t \in [ n ]} \left| \boldsymbol {g} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\xi} \right| ^ {3 k _ {1}} + 1 \geq 2 \left(\frac {\varepsilon}{4}\right) ^ {\frac {3 k _ {1}}{5 k _ {1} + 4}}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq \mathbb {p} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\mathrm {t}} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} \right| ^ {4} \geq \left(\frac {\varepsilon}{4}\right) ^ {\frac {2 k _ {1} + 4}{5 k _ {1} + 4}}\right) \\ + \mathbb {p} \left(\sup  _ {t \in [ n ]} \left| \boldsymbol {g} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\xi} \right| ^ {3 k _ {1}} \geq \left(\frac {\varepsilon}{4}\right) ^ {\frac {3 k _ {1}}{5 k _ {1} + 4}}\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\leq c p \exp \left[ - \min  \left\{\varepsilon^ {\frac {2}{5 k _ {1} + 4}}, (\log p) ^ {2} \right\} / c \right], \tag {235}
$$

where to reach the last step we have used (217) and the standard tails bound for gaussian random variables  $g_{t}^{\top}\pmb{\xi}$ , together with union bound. then, by choosing a large enough  $c$ , we can make (235) hold for any  $\varepsilon \geq 0$ .

(iii)  $\| \frac{1}{p}\sum_{t\neq k}\boldsymbol {r}_t\boldsymbol {r}_t^\top \| \cdot \frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\| \boldsymbol{h}_{\setminus k}^{-1}\boldsymbol {r}\|$ . notice that

$$
\left\| \frac {1}{p} \sum_ {t \neq k} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {t}} \right\| \leq \left\| \frac {1}{p} \sum_ {1 \leq t \leq n} \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {t}} \right\| + \left\| \frac {1}{p} \sum_ {1 \leq t \leq n} \boldsymbol {b} _ {t} \boldsymbol {b} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {t}} \right\|. \tag {236}
$$

from lemma 12, we can then find two constants  $c > 0$  and  $c > 0$  such that

$$
\sup  _ {\boldsymbol {f} \in \mathcal {a} _ {2}} \mathbb {p} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\left\| \frac {1}{p} \sum_ {t \neq k} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \right\| \geq c\right) \leq c e ^ {- p / c}. \tag {237}
$$

moreover, as  $\| h_{\backslash k}^{-1}\| \leq 2 / \lambda$  when  $f\in \mathcal{a}_2$ , we have from lemma 9 that

$$
\sup  _ {\boldsymbol {f} \in \mathcal {a} _ {2}} \mathbb {p} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} \| \geq c\right) \leq c e ^ {- p / c}, \tag {238}
$$

for some  $c, c > 0$ . combining (237), (238), and using lemma 11, we have

$$
\mathbb {p} \left(\| \frac {1}{p} \sum_ {t \neq k} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \| \cdot \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} \| \geq c\right) \leq c e ^ {- p / c}. \tag {239}
$$

(iv)  $\sup_{t\neq k}\{|r_t^\top h_{\backslash k}^{-1}r / p|\}$ . by lemmas 11, 10 and the union bound, we have, for every  $\varepsilon \geq 0$

$$
\mathbb {p} \left(\sup  _ {t \neq k} \left\{\left| \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} / p \right| \right\} \geq \varepsilon\right) \leq c p e ^ {- p \varepsilon^ {2} / c} + c p e ^ {- p / c}. \tag {240}
$$

substituting the bounds (233), (235), (239) and (240) into (231), we have for any  $\varepsilon \geq 0$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {p} \left(\left\| \boldsymbol {w} ^ {*} - \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} \right\| \geq c \varepsilon\right) \\ \leq \mathbb {p} \left(\sup  _ {t \neq k} \left\{\left| \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} / p \right| \right\} \left\| \frac {1}{p} \sum_ {t \neq k} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \right\| \frac {\left\| \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} \right\|}{\sqrt {p}} \geq \frac {(\sqrt {p} \varepsilon) ^ {\frac {2}{5 k _ {1} + 6}}}{2 \sqrt {p}}\right) \\ + \mathbb {p} \left(\left| \ell_ {k} ^ {\prime} \right| ^ {2} l _ {s} \geq (\sqrt {p} \varepsilon) ^ {\frac {5 k _ {1} + 4}{5 k _ {1} + 6}}\right) \\ + \mathbb {p} \left[ \frac {1}{p} \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {p} \left(\boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k, i} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {4}\right) ^ {\frac {1}{2}} \geq \frac {(\sqrt {p} \varepsilon) ^ {\frac {2}{5 k _ {1} + 6}}}{2 \sqrt {p}} \right] \\ \leq c p \exp \left[ - \min  \left\{\left(\sqrt {p} \varepsilon\right) ^ {\frac {2}{5 k _ {1} + 6}}, \left(\sqrt {p} \varepsilon\right) ^ {\frac {4}{5 k _ {1} + 6}}, (\log p) ^ {2} \right\} / c \right], \tag {241} \\ \end{array}
$$

where constant  $c$  does not depend on  $k$  and  $p$ . this completes our proof.

lemma 22. there exists a function  $b(m)$ ,  $m \in \mathbb{z}^+$  such that for every  $\pmb{f} \in \mathcal{a}$ ,  $p \geq 2$  and  $k \in [n]$ ,

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r}) - \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \| ^ {m} \leq b (m) \frac {(\log p) ^ {(2 . 5 k _ {1} + 3) m}}{p ^ {m / 2}}, \tag {242}
$$

where  $\mathbf{r} = \mathbf{a}_k$  or  $\mathbf{b}_k$  and  $k_{1}\in \mathbb{z}^{+}$  is the constant defined in assumption (a.4).

proof. from (196), there exists a function  $b_{1}(m)$  such that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r}) - \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \right\| ^ {m} \\ \leq b _ {1} (m) \left(l _ {s} \left| \ell_ {k} ^ {\prime} \right| ^ {2}\right) ^ {m} \\ \times \left(\sup  _ {t \neq k} \left\{\left| \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} / p \right| \right\} ^ {m} \right\| \frac {1}{p} \sum_ {t \neq k} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \| ^ {m} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} \|\right) ^ {m} \\ \left. + \left[ \frac {1}{p ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {p} \left(\boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k, i} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {4} \right] ^ {m / 2}\right), \tag {243} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\pmb{r} = \pmb{a}_k$  or  $\pmb{b}_k$ . it follows that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {\mathcal {a}} \boldsymbol {f}} \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r}) - \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \| ^ {m} \\ \leq b _ {1} (m) \left[ \mathbb {e} _ {\setminus \boldsymbol {f}} \left(l _ {s} \left| \ell_ {k} ^ {\prime} \right| ^ {2}\right) ^ {4 m} \mathbb {e} _ {\setminus \boldsymbol {f}} \sup  _ {t \neq k} \left\{\left| \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\setminus k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} / p \right| \right\} ^ {4 m} \right. \\ \times \mathbb {e} _ {\backslash f} \left\| \frac {1}{p} \sum_ {t \neq k} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \right\| ^ {4 m} \mathbb {e} _ {\backslash f} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} \|\right) ^ {4 m} \bigg ] ^ {1 / 4} \\ + b _ {1} (m) \left[ \mathbb {e} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left(l _ {s} \left| \ell_ {k} ^ {\prime} \right| ^ {2}\right) ^ {2 m} \mathbb {e} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\frac {1}{p ^ {2}} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {p} \left(\boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k, i} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {4}\right) ^ {m} \right] ^ {1 / 2}, \tag {244} \\ \end{array}
$$

where we have used the following generalized hölder's inequality: for random variables  $x_{1},\ldots ,x_{4}\geq 0$

$\mathbb{e}\left(x_1x_2\cdots x_4\right) \leq \prod_{i=1}^{4}\left(\mathbb{e}x_i^4\right)^{1/4}$ . therefore, to show (242), it suffices to bound each term on the right-hand side of (244). following the same steps leading towards (235), we get there exists  $c > 0$  such that for any  $\varepsilon \geq 0$  and  $\pmb{f} \in \mathcal{a}$ ,

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\setminus \boldsymbol {f}} \left(l _ {s} \left| \ell_ {k} ^ {\prime} \right| ^ {2} \geq \varepsilon\right) \leq c p \exp \left(- \varepsilon^ {\frac {2}{5 k _ {1} + 4}} / c\right).
$$

applying the integral identity  $\mathbb{e}|x| = \int_0^\infty \mathbb{p}(|x| > t)dt$ , we can then show that for  $p \geq 2$ ,  $\mathbb{e}_{\setminus f}(l_s|\ell_k'|^2)^{4m} \leq (\log p)^{(10k_1 + 8)m}b_2(m)$  and  $\mathbb{e}_{\setminus f}(l_s|\ell_k'|^2)^{2m} \leq (\log p)^{(5k_1 + 4)m}b_2(m)$  for some function  $b_2(m)$ . similarly, combining (236) and lemma 12 gives us  $\mathbb{e}_{\setminus f}\left\| \frac{1}{p}\sum_{t \neq k}\boldsymbol{r}_t\boldsymbol{r}_t^\top \right\|^4 m \leq b_3(m)$ . since  $\| h_{\setminus k}^{-1}\| \leq 2 / \lambda$  for  $\boldsymbol{f} \in \mathcal{a}$ , we have  $\mathbb{e}_{\setminus f}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\|\boldsymbol{h}_{\setminus k}^{-1}\boldsymbol{r}\|\right)^{4m} \leq c\mathbb{e}_{\setminus f}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\|\boldsymbol{r}\|\right)^{4m} \leq b_4(m)$ , where the last step is due to (162) and (163).

next, we consider  $\mathbb{e}_{\setminus f}\sup_{t\neq k}\left\{\left|r_t^\top h_{\setminus k}^{-1}r / p\right|\right\}^{4m}$ . applying lemma 10 and the union bound gives us

$$
\mathbb {p} _ {\setminus \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\sup  _ {t \neq k} \{| \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\setminus k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} / p | \} \geq \varepsilon\right) \leq c p e ^ {- p \varepsilon^ {2} / c}.
$$

we can then show that when  $p \geq 2$ ,  $\mathbb{e}_{\setminus f} \sup_{t \neq k} \left\{ |\boldsymbol{r}_t^\top \boldsymbol{h}_{\setminus k}^{-1} \boldsymbol{r} / p| \right\}^{4m} \leq b(m) (\log p / p)^{2m}$  for some function  $b(m)$ . similarly as (233), we can get  $\mathbb{p}_{\setminus f} \left[ \left( \frac{1}{p^2} \sum_{i=1}^{p} (\boldsymbol{h}_{\setminus k,i}^\top \boldsymbol{r})^4 \right)^{\frac{1}{2}} \geq \varepsilon \right] \leq cpe^{-\sqrt{p}\varepsilon / c}$  and then it can be verified that  $\mathbb{e}_{\setminus f} \left( \frac{1}{p^2} \sum_{i=1}^{p} (\boldsymbol{h}_{\setminus k,i}^\top \boldsymbol{r})^4 \right)^m \leq b(m) (\log p / \sqrt{p})^{2m}$ . substituting these bounds in (244), we reach the desired inequality in (242).

# 5) the  $\ell_{\infty}$  boundedness of optimal solutions:

lemma 23. let  $\boldsymbol{w}_k^*$  be the optimal solution to the optimization problem defined in (27). there exists some  $c_{\infty} > 0$  such that for every  $p$  and  $0 \leq k \leq n$ ,

$$
\mathbb {p} \left(\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} \| _ {\infty} \geq (\log p) ^ {3 + 2 k _ {1}}\right) \leq c _ {\infty} \exp \left[ - c _ {\infty} ^ {- 1} (\log p) ^ {2} \right], \tag {245}
$$

where  $k_{1}\in \mathbb{z}^{+}$  is the constant in assumptions (a.4).

proof. the general strategy of our proof is as follows. to bound  $\| \pmb{w}_k^*\|_{\infty}$ , we just need to show that any given coordinate of  $\pmb{w}_k^*$ , e.g., its last entry, is bounded with high probability. by symmetry, all the coordinates have the same marginal distribution. consequently, each coordinate of  $\pmb{w}_k^*$  can be analyzed in the same way and  $\| \pmb{w}_k^*\|_{\infty}$  can then be controlled by using the union bound.

recall that  $\pmb{w}_k^* \in \mathbb{r}^p$ . to simplify the notation, we will instead study a  $(p + 1)$ -dimensional version of the problem in (26) and focus on, without loss of generality, the last coordinate of the optimal solution, denoted by  $u^*$ . let  $\pmb{f}_{p + 1}$  be the new column added to the feature matrix. also define a vector

$$
\boldsymbol {e} \stackrel {\text {d e f}} {=} \left[ b _ {1} \dots b _ {k} a _ {k + 1} \dots a _ {n} \right] ^ {\mathsf {t}}, \tag {246}
$$
