where  $b_{t} = \mu_{1}\pmb{f}_{p + 1}^{\top}\pmb{g}_{t} + \mu_{2}z_{t}$  (with  $z_{t}\sim \mathcal{n}(0,1)$ , independent of  $\pmb{g}_t$ ) and  $a_{t} = \sigma (\pmb{f}_{p + 1}^{\top}\pmb{g}_{t})$ . from (26), the  $(p + 1)$ th coordinate  $u^{*}$  can be expressed as

$$
\begin{array}{l} u ^ {*} = \arg \min  _ {u} \min  _ {\boldsymbol {w}} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {n} \ell \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {w} + e _ {t} u\right); y _ {t}\right) + g (\boldsymbol {w}) \\ + \left(2 \tau_ {1} \mu_ {1} ^ {2} \boldsymbol {f} _ {p + 1} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {f} \boldsymbol {w}\right) u + h (u) \\ + \tau_ {1} \left(\mu_ {1} ^ {2} \left\| \boldsymbol {f} _ {p + 1} \right\| ^ {2} + \mu_ {2} ^ {2}\right) u ^ {2} \\ + \left(\tau_ {2} \mu_ {1} \sqrt {p} \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {\mathrm {t}} \boldsymbol {f} _ {p + 1}\right) u, \tag {247} \\ \end{array}
$$

where

$$
g (\boldsymbol {w}) = q (\boldsymbol {w}) + \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {p} h (w _ {i}).
$$

the rest of the proof consists of two steps. first, we will show

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| u ^ {*} \right| \leq \frac {4}{\lambda} \left| h ^ {\prime} (0) + \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {n} \ell^ {\prime} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*}; y _ {t}\right) e _ {t} + 2 \tau_ {1} \mu_ {1} ^ {2} \boldsymbol {f} _ {p + 1} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {f} \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} \right. \\ + \tau_ {2} \mu_ {1} \sqrt {p} \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {\mathrm {t}} \boldsymbol {f} _ {p + 1} \big |, \tag {248} \\ \end{array}
$$

provided that

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {f} \right\| \leq 1 + 2 \sqrt {\eta} \quad \text {a n d} \quad \left\| \boldsymbol {f} _ {p + 1} \right\| \leq 1 + 2 \sqrt {\eta}. \tag {249}
$$

second, we show that (249) holds with high probability and that each term on the right-hand side of (248) is also bounded with high probability.

we start by proving the bound in (248). let  $\mathcal{l}(u)$  denote the objective function of  $u$  in (247), i.e.,  $u^{*} = \arg \min_{u}\mathcal{l}(u)$ . we first derive a lower bound for  $\mathcal{l}(u)$ . to that end, we note from the convexity of the loss function that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \ell \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w} + e _ {t} u\right); y _ {t}\right) \geq \ell \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*}; y _ {t}\right) + \ell^ {\prime} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*}; y _ {t}\right) \\ \times \left[ \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\mathsf {t}} (\boldsymbol {w} - \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*}) + \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} e _ {t} u \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

moreover, recall from remark 2 that  $g(\omega)$  is  $\frac{\lambda}{2}$ -strongly convex when  $\pmb{f}$  satisfies  $\| \pmb{f} \| \leq 1 + 2\sqrt{\eta}$ . it follows that  $g(\pmb{w}) \geq g(\pmb{w}_k^*) + \nabla^\top g(\pmb{w}_k^*)(\pmb{w} - \pmb{w}_k^*) + \frac{\lambda}{4} \| \pmb{w} - \pmb{w}_k^* \|^2$ . furthermore,  $h(u)$  being  $\lambda$ -strongly convex gives us  $h(u) \geq h(0) + h'(0)u + \frac{\lambda}{2} u^2$ . substituting these inequalities into (247) and using the first-order optimality condition of  $\pmb{w}_k^*$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {l} (u) \geq \mathcal {l} (0) + \min  _ {\boldsymbol {w}} \left\{2 \tau_ {1} \mu_ {1} ^ {2} \boldsymbol {f} _ {p + 1} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {f} (\boldsymbol {w} - \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*}) u + \frac {\lambda}{4} \| \boldsymbol {w} - \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} \| ^ {2} \right\} \\ + \chi u + \frac {\lambda}{2} u ^ {2} \\ = \mathcal {l} (0) + \chi u + \left(\frac {\lambda}{2} - \frac {4}{\lambda} \tau_ {1} ^ {2} \mu_ {1} ^ {4} \boldsymbol {f} _ {p + 1} ^ {\intercal} \boldsymbol {f} \boldsymbol {f} ^ {\intercal} \boldsymbol {f} _ {p + 1}\right) u ^ {2} \\ \geq \mathcal {l} (0) + \chi u + \frac {\lambda}{4} u ^ {2}, \tag {250} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\chi = h'(0) + \frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\ell'(\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\boldsymbol{r}_t^\top\boldsymbol{w}_k^*;y_t)e_t + 2\tau_1\mu_1^2\boldsymbol{f}_{p+1}^\top\boldsymbol{f}\boldsymbol{w}_k^* + \tau_2\mu_1\sqrt{p}\boldsymbol{\xi}^\top\boldsymbol{f}_{p+1}$ . to reach (250), we have used (249) and the constraint (13) on the magnitude of  $\tau_1$ . in the meanwhile, we must have  $\min_u\mathcal{l}(u)\leq \mathcal{l}(0)$ . it follows that  $|u^{*}| = |\arg \min_u\mathcal{l}(u)|\leq \frac{4|\chi|}{\lambda}$  and thus (248).

from (172) and (148), the conditions in (249) hold with probability greater than  $1 - 2e^{-cp}$ , for some  $c > 0$ . thus,

to complete the proof, we just need to bound the following three terms on the right-hand side of (248): (i)  $\sqrt{p}\pmb{\xi}^{\top}\pmb{f}_{p + 1}$ ; (ii)  $\pmb{f}_{p + 1}^{\top}\pmb{f}\pmb{w}_k^*$ ; and (iii)  $\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\sum_{t = 1}^{n}\ell '(\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\pmb{r}_t^\top \pmb{w}_k^*;y_t)e_t$

(i) since  $\sqrt{p}\pmb{\xi}^{\mathsf{t}}\pmb{f}_{p + 1}\sim \mathcal{n}(0,p / d)$ , there exists  $c > 0$  such that

$$
\mathbb {p} \left(\left| \sqrt {p} \boldsymbol {\xi} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {f} _ {p + 1} \right| \geq \log p\right) \leq c e ^ {- (\log p) ^ {2} / c}. \tag {251}
$$

(ii)  $\pmb{f}_{p+1}^{\top} \pmb{f} \pmb{w}_k^*$ . note that  $\pmb{f}_{p+1}$  is independent of  $\pmb{f} \pmb{w}_k^*$ . given  $\pmb{f} \pmb{w}_k^*$ , the conditional distribution of  $\pmb{f}_{p+1}^{\top} \pmb{f} \pmb{w}_k^*$  is  $\mathcal{n}(0, \| \pmb{f} \pmb{w}_k^* \|^2 / d)$ . from (172) and (204), there exists  $c, c > 0$  such that  $\mathbb{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}} \| \pmb{f} \pmb{w}_k^* \| \geq c\right) \leq c e^{-(\log p)^2 / c}$ . it then follows that for some  $c > 0$ ,

$$
\mathbb {p} \left(\left| \boldsymbol {f} _ {p + 1} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {f} \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} \right| \geq \log p\right) <   c e ^ {- (\log p) ^ {2} / c}. \tag {252}
$$

(iii)  $\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\ell'(\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\boldsymbol{r}_t^\top\boldsymbol{w}_k^*;y_t)e_t$ . to simplify the notation, let  $\theta_t^* = \ell'(\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\boldsymbol{r}_t^\top\boldsymbol{w}_k^*;y_t)$ . we first show that  $\theta_t^*$  is bounded with high probability. from inequality (229), there exists a constant  $c > 0$  such that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {p} \left(\left| \theta_ {t} ^ {*} \right| \geq c (\log p) ^ {2 + 2 k _ {1}}\right) \\ \leq \mathbb {p} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} \right| \geq 2 (\log p) ^ {\left(k _ {1} + 2\right) / 2}\right) + \mathbb {p} \left(\left| \boldsymbol {g} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\xi} \right| \geq \log p\right). \tag {253} \\ \end{array}
$$

to bound  $\mathbb{p}\big(|\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\boldsymbol {r}_t^\top \boldsymbol {w}_k^* |\geq 2(\log p)^{(k_1 + 2) / 2}\big)$  in (253), we consider different  $t$  . when  $1\leq t\leq k$  , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {p} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} \right| \geq 2 (\log p) ^ {\frac {k _ {1} + 2}{2}}\right) \\ = \mathbb {p} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {b} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} \right| \geq 2 (\log p) ^ {\frac {k _ {1} + 2}{2}}\right) \\ = \mathbb {p} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {b} _ {k} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} \right| \geq 2 (\log p) ^ {\frac {k _ {1} + 2}{2}}\right) \\ \leq \mathbb {p} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {b} _ {k} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {b} _ {k}) \right| \geq (\log p) ^ {\frac {k _ {1} + 2}{2}}\right) \\ + \mathbb {p} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {b} _ {k} \| \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} - \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {b} _ {k}) \| \geq (\log p) ^ {\frac {k _ {1} + 2}{2}}\right) \\ = \mathbb {p} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {b} _ {k} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {b} _ {k}) \right| \geq (\log p) ^ {\frac {k _ {1} + 2}{2}}\right) \\ + \mathbb {p} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {b} _ {k} \| \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {b} _ {k}) - \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {b} _ {k}) \| \geq (\log p) ^ {\frac {k _ {1} + 2}{2}}\right), \tag {254} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second equality is due to symmetry of  $\pmb{b}_t, 1 \leq t \leq k$ . similarly, when  $k < t \leq n$ , we can get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {p} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} \right| \geq 2 (\log p) ^ {\frac {k _ {1} + 2}{2}}\right) \\ \leq \mathbb {p} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {a} _ {k + 1} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k + 1} (\boldsymbol {a} _ {k + 1}) \right| \geq (\log p) ^ {\frac {k _ {1} + 2}{2}}\right) + \\ \mathbb {p} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {a} _ {k + 1} \| \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k + 1} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {a} _ {k + 1}) - \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k + 1} (\boldsymbol {a} _ {k + 1}) \| \geq (\log p) ^ {\frac {k _ {1} + 2}{2}}\right). \tag {255} \\ \end{array}
$$

by lemma 19, there exists  $c > 0$  such that

$$
\mathbb {p} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} _ {k} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {r} _ {k}) \right| \geq (\log p) ^ {\frac {k _ {1} + 2}{2}}\right) \leq c e ^ {- (\log p) ^ {2} / c},
$$

for  $\pmb{r}_k = \pmb{a}_a$  or  $\pmb{b}_k$ . moreover, there exists  $c, c > 0$  such that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {p} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {r} _ {k} \| \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r} _ {k}) - \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {r} _ {k}) \| \geq (\log p) ^ {\frac {k _ {1} + 2}{2}}\right) \\ \leq \mathbb {p} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {r} _ {k} \| \geq c\right) + \mathbb {p} \left(\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r} _ {k}) - \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {r} _ {k}) \| \geq \frac {(\log p) ^ {\frac {k _ {1} + 2}{2}}}{c}\right) \\ \leq c e ^ {- (\log p) ^ {2} / c}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where in reaching the last step we have used (230), lemma 11 and lemma 9. substituting these two bounds into (254) and (255), we get there exists  $c > 0$  such that for every  $1 \leq t \leq k$ ,

$$
\mathbb {p} \left(\left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\mathrm {t}} \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} \right| \geq 2 (\log p) ^ {\frac {k _ {1} + 2}{2}}\right) \leq c e ^ {- (\log p) ^ {2} / c}. \tag {256}
$$

on the other hand, since  $\pmb{g}_t^\top \pmb {\xi}\sim \mathcal{n}(0,1)$  , we have

$$
\mathbb {p} \left(\left| \boldsymbol {g} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\xi} \right| \geq \log p\right) \leq c e ^ {- (\log p) ^ {2} / c}. \tag {257}
$$

therefore, from (253), (256) and (257), we get there exists  $c > 0$  such that for any  $p \in \mathbb{z}^+$ ,

$$
\mathbb {p} \left(\left| \theta_ {t} ^ {*} \right| \geq c (\log p) ^ {2 + 2 k _ {1}}\right) \leq c e ^ {- (\log p) ^ {2} / c}. \tag {258}
$$

recall the definition of  $e_t$  in (246). we have

$$
\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \sum_ {t} \theta_ {t} ^ {*} e _ {t} = u (\sqrt {d} \boldsymbol {f} _ {p + 1}) + \frac {\mu_ {2}}{\sqrt {p}} \sum_ {t \leq k} \theta_ {t} ^ {*} z _ {t}, \tag {259}
$$

where  $u:\mathbb{r}^d\mapsto \mathbb{r}$  is a function defined as

$$
u (\boldsymbol {x}) \stackrel {\text {d e f}} {=} \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \sum_ {t \leq k} \theta_ {t} ^ {*} \mu_ {1} \frac {1}{\sqrt {d}} \boldsymbol {g} _ {t} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {x} + \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \sum_ {k + 1 \leq t \leq n} \theta_ {t} ^ {*} \sigma \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {d}} \boldsymbol {g} _ {t} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {x}\right). \tag {260}
$$

let us consider the following event

$$
e = \left\{\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| \leq c (\log p) ^ {2 + 2 k _ {1}}, \frac {1}{\sqrt {d}} \| \boldsymbol {g} \| \leq k \right\},
$$

where  $\pmb{\theta}^{*} = [\theta_{1}^{*},\theta_{1}^{*},\dots,\theta_{n}^{*}]^{\mathrm{t}}$ $c$  is the constant in (258),  $\pmb {g} = [g_1g_2\dots g_n]^{\mathrm{t}}$  is the matrix of the latent input vectors in assumption (a.1), and  $k$  is some sufficiently large constant. notice that  $e$  is a high probability event. indeed, from (171) and (258), there exists  $c > 0$  such that for every  $k$  large enough,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {p} \left(e ^ {c}\right) \leq \mathbb {p} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {\theta} ^ {*} \| > c (\log p) ^ {2 + 2 k _ {1}}\right) + \mathbb {p} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {g} \| > k\right) \\ \leq c e ^ {- (\log p) ^ {2} / c}. \\ \end{array}
$$

(261)

conditioned on any  $g$  and  $\pmb{f}$  in  $e$ , the two terms of the right-hand side of (259) can be easily bounded. specifically, let

$$
j = \frac {\lambda}{3 2} (\log p) ^ {3 + 2 k _ {1}}. \tag {262}
$$

since  $\{z_t\}$  is a set of i.i.d. standard normal random variables independent of  $\theta_t^*$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {p} \big (\left| \frac {\mu_ {2}}{\sqrt {p}} \sum_ {t \leq k} \theta_ {t} ^ {*} z _ {t} \right| \geq j \big) \\ \leq \mathbb {p} \left(\left\{\left| \frac {\mu_ {2}}{\sqrt {p}} \sum_ {t \leq k} \theta_ {t} ^ {*} z _ {t} \right| \geq j \right\} \cap e\right) + \mathbb {p} (e ^ {c}) \\ \leq c e ^ {- \left(\log p\right) ^ {2} / c}, \tag {263} \\ \end{array}
$$

where in reach the last inequality we have used the standard tail bound for gaussian random variables. to bound the first term on the right-hand side of (259), we note that, given any  $\pmb{g}$  and  $\pmb{f}$  in  $e$ , the function  $u(\pmb{x})$  in (260) is a lipschitz continuous mapping with a lipschitz constant  $ck(\log p)^{2 + 2k_1}$  for some constant  $c > 0$ . since  $\sqrt{d}\pmb{f}_{p + 1}$  is a standard gaussian vector and  $\mathbb{e}_{\backslash g,f}\left[u(\sqrt{d}\pmb{f}_{p + 1})\right] = 0$ , we can apply (152) to get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {p} \left(\left| u (\sqrt {d} \boldsymbol {f} _ {p + 1}) \right| \geq j\right) \\ \leq \mathbb {p} \left(\left\{\left| u (\sqrt {d} \boldsymbol {f} _ {p + 1}) \right| \geq j \right\} \cap e\right) + \mathbb {p} (e ^ {c}) \\ \leq c e ^ {- (\log p) ^ {2} / c}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where in the last step we have used the specific value of  $j$  in (262). combining this inequality and (263), we can then get from (259) that  $\mathbb{p}\left(\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\sum_{t}\theta_{t}^{*}e_{t}\right|\geq \frac{\lambda}{16} (\log p)^{3 + 2k_{1}}\right) < ce^{-(\log p)^{2} / c}$ . finally, substituting this bound, (251), and (252) into (248), we have

$$
\mathbb {p} \left[ \left| u ^ {*} \right| \geq (\log p) ^ {3 + 2 k _ {1}} \right] \leq c e ^ {- (\log p) ^ {2} / c}.
$$

since  $u^{*}$  is the last coordinate of the optimal weight vector, and since all the coordinates have the same distribution by symmetry, we get from the union bound that

$$
\mathbb {p} \left[ \left| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} \right| \geq \left(\log p\right) ^ {3 + 2 k _ {1}}\right) ] \leq c p e ^ {- (\log p) ^ {2} / c}.
$$

note that there exists  $p_0$  such that for any  $p \geq p_0$ ,  $cpe^{-(\log p)^2 /c} \leq 2ce^{-(\log p)^2 /(2c)}$ . we can get (245) by choosing  $c_{\infty}$  to be the smallest number satisfying  $c_{\infty} \geq 2c$  and  $c_{\infty}e^{-(\log p_0)^2 /c_{\infty}} \geq 1$ .

6) proof of proposition 2: we write  $\mathcal{a}_3$  as  $\mathcal{a}_3 = \cap_{k=1}^{n} \mathcal{a}_{3,k}$ , where

$$
\mathcal {a} _ {3, k} \stackrel {\text {d e f}} {=} \left\{\boldsymbol {f}: \mathbb {e} _ {\setminus \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} \| _ {\infty} ^ {2}\right) \leq (\log p) ^ {7 + 4 k _ {1}} \right\}.
$$

to show (30), it suffices to show that each  $\mathcal{a}_{3,k}$  has high probability. consider the following set of  $\pmb{f}$ :

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathcal {b} _ {k} \stackrel {\text {d e f}} {=} \left\{\boldsymbol {f}: \mathbb {p} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} \| _ {\infty} \leq (\log p) ^ {3 + 2 k _ {1}}\right) \right. \\ \left. \geq 1 - c _ {\infty} e ^ {- (\log p) ^ {2} / (2 c _ {\infty})} \right\}, \tag {264} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $c_{\infty}$  is the constant in (245). from (245), we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} 1 - c _ {\infty} e ^ {- (\log p) ^ {2} / c _ {\infty}} \\ \leq \mathbb {p} \left(\left\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\infty} \leq (\log p) ^ {3 + 2 k _ {1}}\right) \\ = \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {f}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {\mathcal {b} _ {k}} \mathbb {p} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} \| _ {\infty} \leq (\log p) ^ {3 + 2 k _ {1}}\right) \right] \\ + \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {f}} \left[ \mathbb {1} _ {\mathcal {b} _ {k} ^ {c}} \mathbb {p} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} \| _ {\infty} \leq (\log p) ^ {3 + 2 k _ {1}}\right) \right] \\ \leq \mathbb {p} \left(\mathcal {b} _ {k}\right) + \left[ 1 - \mathbb {p} \left(\mathcal {b} _ {k}\right) \right] \left[ 1 - c _ {\infty} e ^ {- (\log p) ^ {2} / \left(2 c _ {\infty}\right)} \right], \\ \end{array}
$$

which indicates that

$$
\mathbb {p} \left(\mathcal {b} _ {k}\right) \geq 1 - e ^ {- (\log p) ^ {2} / \left(2 c _ {\infty}\right)}.
$$

let  $\mathcal{a}_2$  be the set defined in (23). from lemma 18, we know there exists  $c > 0$  such that, for every  $f \in \mathcal{a}_2$ ,  $p \geq 2$  and  $0 \leq k \leq n$ ,

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\left\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} \right\| ^ {4}\right) \leq c p ^ {2} (\log p) ^ {2 k _ {1}}. \tag {265}
$$

therefore, for every  $f \in \mathcal{a}_2 \cap \mathcal{b}_k$ , it holds that for  $p \geq 2$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} _ {\backslash f} \left\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\infty} ^ {2} \\ = \mathbb {e} _ {\mathbf {f}} \left(\mathbb {1} _ {\left\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\infty} \leq (\log p) ^ {3 + 2 k _ {1}}} \left\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\infty} ^ {2}\right) \\ + \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {f}} \left(\mathbb {1} _ {\left\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\infty} > (\log p) ^ {3 + 2 k _ {1}}} \left\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\infty} ^ {2}\right) \\ \leq (\log p) ^ {6 + 4 k _ {1}} + \mathbb {e} _ {\setminus f} \left(\mathbb {1} _ {\left\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} \right\| _ {\infty} > (\log p) ^ {3 + 2 k _ {1}}} \left\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} \right\| ^ {2}\right) \\ \leq (\log p) ^ {6 + 4 k _ {1}} + \sqrt {\mathbb {e} _ {\backslash f} \left(\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} \| ^ {4}\right)} \times \\ \sqrt {\mathbb {p} _ {\backslash f} \left(\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} \| _ {\infty} > (\log p) ^ {3 + 2 k _ {1}}\right)} \\ \leq \left(\log p\right) ^ {6 + 4 k _ {1}} + c p \left(\log p\right) ^ {k _ {1}} e ^ {- \left(\log p\right) ^ {2} / c}, \tag {266} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $c > 0$  is some constant, and we have used (265) and (264) in reaching the last step. there exists a constant  $p_0$  such that for any  $p \geq p_0$ , the right-hand side of (266) is bounded by  $(\log p)^{7 + 4k_1}$  and in that case,  $\mathcal{a}_2 \cap \mathcal{b}_k \subset \mathcal{a}_{3,k}$ . since there exists  $c_1 > 0$  such that  $\mathbb{p}(\mathcal{a}_2) \geq 1 - c_1 e^{-p/c}$  and  $\mathbb{p}(\mathcal{b}_k) \geq 1 - e^{-(\log p)^2/(c_1)}$ , we know there exists some  $c_2 > 0$  such that  $\mathbb{p}(\mathcal{a}_{3,k}) \geq 1 - c_2 e^{-(\log p)^2/c_2}$  for every  $p \geq p_0$  and  $0 \leq k \leq n$ . choose a large enough constant  $c$  satisfying  $c \geq c_2$  and  $ce^{-(\log p_0)^2/c} \geq 1$ , we then have

$$
\mathbb {p} \left(\mathcal {a} _ {3, k}\right) \geq 1 - c e ^ {- (\log p) ^ {2} / c},
$$

for every  $p$  and  $0 \leq k \leq n$ . finally, (30) can be obtained by applying the union bound.

7) proof of lemma 1: recall the definitions of  $\phi_k(r)$  and  $\psi_k(r)$  in (188) and (190) of appendix f. the corresponding optimal solutions  $\widetilde{w}_k(r)$  and  $w_{k}^{*}(r)$  are also defined in (188) and (190), respectively. we first show (36). let  $\pmb {r} = \pmb{a}_{k}$  or  $\pmb{b}_{k}$ . it follows from (191) that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \left(\psi_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) - \phi_ {\backslash k}\right) ^ {2} = \mathbb {e} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \mathcal {m} _ {k} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}; \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r})\right) ^ {2} \\ \leq \mathbb {e} _ {\backslash f} \ell \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}; y _ {k}\right) ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {(a)} {\leq} \mathbb {e} _ {\mathbf {\backslash} \boldsymbol {f}} q \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} \|\right) \\ \begin{array}{l} \stackrel {(b)} {\leq} \text {p o l y l o g} p, \end{array} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $q(x)$  in step (a) is some finite degree polynomial. to reach (a), we have used (229) and lemma 8 and (b) follows from (213).

we now move on to showing (37). by applying taylor expansion,  $r_{k}(\boldsymbol{w}; \boldsymbol{r})$  in (186) can be written as

$$
\begin{array}{l} r _ {k} (\boldsymbol {w}; \boldsymbol {r}) = \phi_ {\backslash k} + \frac {1}{2} \left(\boldsymbol {w} - \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}\right) ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} \left(\boldsymbol {w} - \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}\right) \\ + \frac {1}{6} \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {k - 1} \ell^ {\prime \prime \prime} (\nu_ {t}; y _ {t}) \left[ \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {b} _ {t} ^ {\mathsf {t}} (\boldsymbol {w} - \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}) \right] ^ {3} \\ + \frac {1}{6} \sum_ {t = k + 1} ^ {n} \ell^ {\prime \prime \prime} (\nu_ {t}; y _ {t}) \left[ \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {w} - \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}) \right] ^ {3} \\ + \frac {1}{6} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {p} h ^ {\prime \prime \prime} \left(w _ {i} ^ {\prime}\right) \left(w _ {i} - w _ {\backslash k, i} ^ {*}\right) ^ {3} + \ell \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w}; y _ {k}\right), \tag {267} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $h_{\backslash k}$  is the hessian matrix defined in (35),  $\nu_{t}$  denotes some point that lies between  $\frac{\boldsymbol{r}_t^{\mathrm{t}}\boldsymbol{w}}{\sqrt{p}}$  and  $\frac{\boldsymbol{r}_t^{\mathrm{t}}\boldsymbol{w}_{\backslash k}^*}{\sqrt{p}}$ , with  $\boldsymbol{r}_t = \boldsymbol{a}_t$  or  $\boldsymbol{b}_t$ ,  $t \neq k$  and  $w_i'$  denotes some point that lies between  $w_i$  and  $w_{\backslash k,i}^*$ . by recalling the definition of  $s_k(\boldsymbol{w};\boldsymbol{r})$

in (187) and that of  $l_{s}$  in (197), we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| r _ {k} (\boldsymbol {w}; \boldsymbol {r}) - s _ {k} (\boldsymbol {w}; \boldsymbol {r}) \right| \\ \leq c _ {1} l _ {s} \left(\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {k - 1} \left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {b} _ {t} ^ {\mathsf {t}} (\boldsymbol {w} - \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}) \right| ^ {3} + \sum_ {t = k + 1} ^ {n} \left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} ^ {\mathsf {t}} (\boldsymbol {w} - \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}) \right| ^ {3}\right) \\ + c _ {1} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {p} \left| w _ {i} - w _ {\backslash k, i} ^ {*} \right| ^ {3} \\ \leq c _ {2} l _ {s} \left(\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {k - 1} \left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {b} _ {t} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \left(\boldsymbol {w} - \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {r})\right) \right| ^ {3} \right. \\ \left. + \sum_ {t = k + 1} ^ {n} \left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} ^ {\top} (\boldsymbol {w} - \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {r})) \right| ^ {3}\right) \\ + c _ {2} l _ {s} \left(\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {k - 1} \left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {b} _ {t} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \left(\tilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) - \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}\right) \right| ^ {3} \right. \\ \left. + \sum_ {t = k + 1} ^ {n} \left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \left(\widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) - \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*}\right) \right| ^ {3}\right) \\ + c _ {2} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {p} \left(\left| w _ {i} - \widetilde {w} _ {i} (\boldsymbol {r}) \right| ^ {3} + \left| \widetilde {w} _ {i} (\boldsymbol {r}) - w _ {\{k, i \}} ^ {*} \right| ^ {3}\right), \tag {268} \\ \end{array}
$$

for some constants  $c_1, c_2 > 0$ , where the first step is obtained similar as (202).

let  $\mathcal{b} = \{\pmb{w}_k^*(\pmb{r})\} \cup \{\widetilde{\pmb{w}}_k(\pmb{r})\}$ . it is easy to verify that

$$
\begin{array}{l} | \phi_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) - \psi_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) | = | \min  _ {\boldsymbol {w} \in \mathcal {b}} r _ {k} (\boldsymbol {w}; \boldsymbol {r}) - \min  _ {\boldsymbol {w} \in \mathcal {b}} s _ {k} (\boldsymbol {w}; \boldsymbol {r}) | \\ \leq \max  _ {\boldsymbol {w} \in \mathcal {b}} | r _ {k} (\boldsymbol {w}; \boldsymbol {r}) - s _ {k} (\boldsymbol {w}; \boldsymbol {r}) |. \tag {269} \\ \end{array}
$$

this then allows us to apply (268) to get

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \phi_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) - \psi_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \right| \\ \leq c l _ {s} \left\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r}) - \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \right\| ^ {3} \left[ \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {k - 1} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {b} _ {t} \|\right) ^ {3} \right. \\ \left. + \sum_ {t = k + 1} ^ {n} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} \|\right) ^ {3} \right] \\ + c l _ {s} \left| \ell_ {k} ^ {\prime} \right| ^ {3} \left(\sum_ {t = 1} ^ {k - 1} \left| \frac {1}{p} \boldsymbol {b} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} \right| ^ {3} + \sum_ {t = k + 1} ^ {n} \left| \frac {1}{p} \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} \right| ^ {3}\right) \\ + c \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {p} \left(\left\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r}) - \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \right\| ^ {3} + \left| \ell_ {k} ^ {\prime} \right| ^ {3} \left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} h _ {\backslash k, i} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r} \right| ^ {3}\right), \tag {270} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $c > 0$ ,  $\ell_k' \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \ell' \left( \frac{\boldsymbol{r}^\top \widetilde{\boldsymbol{w}}_k(\boldsymbol{r})}{\sqrt{p}}; y_k \right)$ ,  $\boldsymbol{h}_{\backslash k,i}$  is the  $i$ th column of  $\boldsymbol{h}_{\backslash k}^{-1}$  and we have used (192). using the simple inequality

$(\sum_{i = 1}^{n}|a_{i}|)^{2}\leq n\sum_{i = 1}^{n}a_{i}^{2},$  we then have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \phi_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) - \psi_ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \right| ^ {2} \\ \leq c p l _ {s} ^ {2} \left\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r}) - \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \right\| ^ {6} \\ \times \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {n} \left[ \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {b} _ {t} \|\right) ^ {6} + \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} \|\right) ^ {6} + 1 \right] \\ + c p l _ {s} ^ {2} \left| \ell_ {k} ^ {\prime} \right| ^ {6} \sum_ {t \neq k} \left(\left| \frac {1}{p} \boldsymbol {b} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} \right| ^ {6} + \left| \frac {1}{p} \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} \right| ^ {6}\right) \\ + c p \left| \ell_ {k} ^ {\prime} \right| ^ {6} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {p} \left| \frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k, i} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r} \right| ^ {6}. \tag {271} \\ \end{array}
$$

therefore, it suffices to control the expectation of each term on the right-hand side of (271), which can be done as follows. similar to what we did in reaching (235), we can get there exists  $c > 0$  such that for any  $\varepsilon > 0$ ,  $\mathbb{p}(l_s \geq \varepsilon) \leq cp\exp(-\varepsilon^{2/k_1}/c)$ , which implies  $\mathbb{e}_{\setminus f}l_s^8 \leq \text{polylog } p$  by the identity  $\mathbb{e}|x| = \int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{p}(|x| > t)dt$ . also from (242), we have  $\mathbb{e}_{\setminus f}\|\pmb{w}_k^*(\pmb{r}) - \widetilde{\pmb{w}}_k(\pmb{r})\|^{24} \leq \frac{\text{polylog } p}{p^{12}}$ . hence  $\mathbb{e}_{\setminus f}l_s^4\|\pmb{w}_k^*(\pmb{r}) - \widetilde{\pmb{w}}_k(\pmb{r})\|^{12} \leq \frac{\text{polylog } p}{p^6}$ . from lemma 9, we have  $\mathbb{e}_{\setminus f}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\|\pmb{a}_t\|\right)^{12} \leq c$  and  $\mathbb{e}_{\setminus f}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\|\pmb{b}_t\|\right)^{12} \leq c$ . it follows from hölder's inequality that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} _ {\setminus f} \left[ l _ {s} ^ {2} \left\| \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} (\boldsymbol {r}) - \widetilde {\boldsymbol {w}} _ {k} (\boldsymbol {r}) \right\| ^ {6} \right. \\ \left. \times \sum_ {t = 1} ^ {n} \left(\left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {b} _ {t} \|\right) ^ {6} + \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {a} _ {t} \|\right) ^ {6} + 1\right) \right] \\ \leq \frac {\text {p o l y l o g} p}{p ^ {2}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

the other terms in (271) can be bounded similarly. from (226), we have  $\mathbb{e}_{\setminus f}|\ell_k'|^{24}\leq \mathrm{polylog}p$ . also we have obtained  $\mathbb{e}_{\setminus f}l_s^8\leq \mathrm{polylog}p$ . therefore,  $\mathbb{e}_{\setminus f}l_s^4 |\ell_k'|^{12}\leq$  polylog  $p$ . combining lemma 10 and (154), we can get  $\mathbb{e}_{\setminus f}\left|\frac{1}{p}\boldsymbol {r}_t^\top h_{\setminus k}^{-1}\boldsymbol {r}\right|^{12}\leq \frac{c}{p^6}$  for  $t\neq k$ , with  $\boldsymbol {r}_t = \boldsymbol {a}_t$  or  $\boldsymbol {b}_t$ . finally, for  $\boldsymbol {f}\in \mathcal{a}$ , we have  $\| h_{\setminus k}^{-1}\| \leq 2 / \lambda$  and thus  $\| h_{\setminus k,i}\| \leq 2 / \lambda$ , ( $h_{\setminus k,i}$  is the  $i$ th column of  $h_{\setminus k}^{-1}$ ). we can then apply lemma 8 to get  $\mathbb{e}_{\setminus f}\left|\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}} h_{\setminus k,i}^\top r\right|^{12}\leq \frac{c}{p^6}$ . substituting the above bounds into (271), we reach the claim (37) of the lemma.

8) two auxiliary lemmas for proving theorem 1:

lemma 24. let  $\delta_1$  and  $\delta_2$  be the quantities defined in (45) and (46), respectively. it holds that  $\delta_1 \leq \frac{\text{polylog } p}{\sqrt{p}}$  and  $\delta_2 \leq \frac{\text{polylog } p}{\sqrt{p}}$ , uniformly over  $\mathbf{f} \in \mathcal{a}$  and  $k \in [n]$ .

proof. from (41) we can get

$$
\frac {\partial \mathcal {m} _ {k} (z ; \gamma)}{\partial \gamma} = - \frac {1}{2} \ell^ {\prime} \left(\operatorname {p r o x} _ {k} (z; \gamma); y _ {k}\right) ^ {2}. \tag {272}
$$

to bound the right-hand side of (272), first note from (222) that

$$
\left| \operatorname {p r o x} _ {k} (z; \gamma) \right| \leq \sqrt {2 \gamma \ell (0 ; y _ {k})} + | z | \leq \gamma + \ell (0; y _ {k}) + | z |. \tag {273}
$$

thus, under assumptions (a.4), there exists  $c_1, c_2 > 0$  such that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \ell^ {\prime} \left(\operatorname {p r o x} _ {k} (z; \gamma); y _ {k}\right) \\ \leq c _ {1} \left(\left| \operatorname {p r o x} _ {k} (z; \gamma) \right| ^ {2} + 1\right) \left(\left| s _ {k} \right| ^ {k _ {1}} + 1\right) \\ \leq c _ {2} \left(\gamma^ {2} + \ell \left(0; y _ {k}\right) ^ {2} + | z | ^ {2} + 1\right) \left(| s _ {k} | ^ {k _ {1}} + 1\right), \tag {274} \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $y_{k} = \theta_{\mathrm{teach}}(s_{k})$  and  $s_k = g_k^\top \pmb {\xi}\sim \mathcal{n}(0,1)$  and the first inequality follows from (229). from (272), there exists  $c > 0$  such that for any  $\gamma^\prime$  between  $\gamma_k(\pmb {b}_k)$  and  $\gamma_{k}$

$$
\left| \frac {\partial \mathcal {m} _ {k} (z ; \gamma^ {\prime})}{\partial \gamma} \right| \leq c \left(\gamma_ {k} ^ {4} + \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {b} _ {k}) ^ {4} + \ell (0; y _ {k}) ^ {4} + | z | ^ {4} + 1\right) \left(| s _ {k} | ^ {2 k _ {1}} + 1\right)
$$

then using (181), (158) and assumption (a.4), we can get

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {k} \left(\frac {\partial \mathcal {m} _ {k} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {b} _ {k} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} ; \gamma\right)}{\partial \gamma}\right) ^ {2} \leq q \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} \|\right), \tag {275}
$$

where  $q(x)$  is a finite degree polynomial. therefore, for some  $\gamma^\prime$  between  $\gamma_{k}(\pmb{b}_{k})$  and  $\gamma_{k}$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \delta_ {1} \leq \mathbb {e} _ {\boldsymbol {\mathcal {f}}} \mathbb {e} _ {k} \left\{\left| \frac {\partial \mathcal {m} _ {k} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {b} _ {k} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} ; \gamma^ {\prime}\right)}{\partial \gamma} \right| \left| \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {b} _ {k}) - \gamma_ {k} \right| \right\} \\ \stackrel {(a)} {\leq} \mathbb {e} _ {\mathbf {\psi} _ {f}} \left\{\sqrt {q \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} \|\right)} \sqrt {\mathbb {e} _ {k} \left[ \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {b} _ {k}) - \gamma_ {k} \right] ^ {2}} \right\} \\ \stackrel {(b)} {\leq} \frac {c _ {1}}{\sqrt {p}} \sqrt {\mathbb {e} _ {\mathbf {\psi} \backslash \mathbf {f}} q \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \mathbf {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} \|\right)} \\ \stackrel {(c)} {\leq} \frac {\mathrm {p o l y l o g} p}{\sqrt {p}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $c_1 > 0$  is some constant. here, (a) follows from (275); in (b), we use (180); in (c), we use (212).

the term  $\delta_{2}$  can be bounded similarly. following the same steps as above, we can show there exists some polynomial  $q(x)$  such that

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {k} \left(\frac {\partial \mathcal {m} _ {k} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {a} _ {k} ^ {\mathrm {t}} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} ; \gamma\right)}{\partial \gamma}\right) ^ {2} \leq q \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} \|\right) \tag {276}
$$

for any  $\gamma^\prime$  between  $\gamma_k(\pmb {a}_k)$  and  $\gamma_{k}$  . it follows that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \delta_ {2} \leq \mathbb {e} _ {\backslash \boldsymbol {f}} \mathbb {e} _ {k} \left[ \left| \frac {\partial \mathcal {m} _ {k} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {a} _ {k} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} ; \gamma^ {\prime}\right)}{\partial \gamma} \right| \left| \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {a} _ {k}) - \gamma_ {k} \right| \right] \\ \leq \mathbb {e} _ {\backslash f} \mathbb {e} _ {k} \left[ \right.\left| \frac {\partial \mathcal {m} _ {k} \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \boldsymbol {a} _ {k} ^ {\mathrm {t}} \boldsymbol {w} _ {k} ^ {*} ; \gamma^ {\prime}\right)}{\partial \gamma} \right|\left(\left| \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {a} _ {k}) - \mathbb {e} _ {k} \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {a} _ {k}) \right|\right. \\ \left. + \left| \mathbb {e} _ {k} \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {a} _ {k}) - \gamma_ {k} \right|\right) \bigg ] \\ \leq \sqrt {\mathbb {e} _ {\setminus \boldsymbol {f}} \left[ q \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {\setminus k} ^ {*} \|\right) \mathbb {e} _ {k} \left| \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {a} _ {k}) - \mathbb {e} _ {k} \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {a} _ {k}) \right| ^ {2} \right]} \\ + \sqrt {\mathbb {e} _ {\backslash f} \left[ q \left(\frac {1}{\sqrt {p}} \| \boldsymbol {w} _ {\backslash k} ^ {*} \|\right) \left| \mathbb {e} _ {k} \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {a} _ {k}) - \gamma_ {k} \right| ^ {2} \right]}, \tag {277} \\ \end{array}
$$

where in the last step we use (276) and hölder's inequality. we need to bound the term  $\left|\mathbb{e}_k\gamma_k(\pmb {a}_k) - \gamma_k\right|$  in (277). recall that  $\gamma_{k} = \mathbb{e}_{k}\gamma_{k}(\pmb{b}_{k})$ . thus,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \mathbb {e} _ {k} \gamma_ {k} (\boldsymbol {a} _ {k}) - \gamma_ {k} \right| = \frac {1}{p} \left| \mathbb {e} _ {k} \left(\boldsymbol {a} _ {k} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {a} _ {k} - \boldsymbol {b} _ {k} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {b} _ {k}\right) \right| \\ = \frac {1}{p} \left| \operatorname {t r} \left[ \boldsymbol {h} _ {\backslash k} ^ {- 1} \left(\boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {a} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {b}\right) \right] \right| \\ \leq \frac {\operatorname {p o l y} \log p}{\sqrt {p}}, \tag {278} \\ \end{array}
$$

where in the last step, we use lemma 5 and the fact that  $\| h_{\backslash k}^{-1}\| \leq \frac{2}{\lambda}$  for  $f\in \mathcal{a}$ . plugging (278), (178) and (213) into (277), we conclude that  $\delta_2\leq \frac{\mathrm{polylog}p}{\sqrt{p}}$

lemma 25. there exists a function  $b(s)$  such that  $\mathbb{e}b^4 (z) < \infty$  for  $z\sim \mathcal{n}(0,1)$  and for each  $k\in [n]$ ,

$$
\left. \max  \left\{\mathcal {m} _ {k} (x; \gamma_ {k}), \mathcal {m} _ {k} ^ {\prime} (x; \gamma_ {k}) \right\} \leq b \left(\boldsymbol {g} _ {k} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\xi}\right) \left(1 + | x | ^ {3}\right). \right. \tag {279}
$$

proof. from (41), we can verify that

$$
\mathcal {m} _ {k} (x; \gamma_ {k}) \leq \ell (x; y _ {k}) \tag {280}
$$

and

$$
\mathcal {m} _ {k} ^ {\prime} (x; \gamma_ {k}) = \ell^ {\prime} (\operatorname {p r o x} _ {k} (x; \gamma_ {k}); y _ {k}), \tag {281}
$$

where  $\operatorname{prox}_k(x;\gamma_k)$  is the proximal operator of  $\ell (x;y_k)$ . moreover, from (222),

$$
| \operatorname {p r o x} _ {k} (x; \gamma_ {k}) | \leq \gamma_ {k} + \ell (0; y _ {k}) + | x |. \tag {282}
$$

combining (280), (282) with assumption (a.4) allows us to show that  $\mathcal{m}_k(x;\gamma_k)$  satisfies (279). indeed, similar as (229), we can get

$$
\ell (x; y _ {k}) \leq c _ {1} \left(| x | ^ {3} + 1\right) \left(\left| \boldsymbol {g} _ {k} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\xi} \right| ^ {k _ {1}} + 1\right), \tag {283}
$$

for some  $c_1 > 0$ . then from (280) and (283), there exists  $c > 0$  such that

$$
\mathcal {m} _ {k} (x; \gamma_ {k}) \leq (| x | ^ {3} + 1) \underbrace {c \left(\left| \boldsymbol {g} _ {k} ^ {\mathrm {t}} \boldsymbol {\xi} \right| ^ {k _ {1}} + 1\right)} _ {b _ {1} \left(\boldsymbol {g} _ {k} ^ {\mathrm {t}} \boldsymbol {\xi}\right)}. \tag {284}
$$

similarly, there exist  $c_1', c_2', c_3' > 0$  such that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \mathcal {m} _ {k} ^ {\prime} (x; \gamma_ {k}) \right| \stackrel {(a)} {\leq} c _ {1} ^ {\prime} \left(\left| \operatorname {p r o x} _ {k} (x; \gamma_ {k}) \right| ^ {2} + 1\right) \left(\left| \boldsymbol {g} _ {k} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {\xi} \right| ^ {k _ {1}} + 1\right) \\ \stackrel {(b)} {\leq} c _ {2} ^ {\prime} \left(\gamma_ {k} ^ {2} + \ell \left(0; y _ {k}\right) ^ {2} + | x | ^ {2} + 1\right) \left(| \boldsymbol {g} _ {k} ^ {\mathsf {t}} \boldsymbol {\xi} | ^ {k _ {1}} + 1\right) \\ \stackrel {(c)} {\leq} \left(| x | ^ {3} + 1\right) \underbrace {c _ {3} ^ {\prime} \left(\left| g _ {k} ^ {\mathrm {t}} \boldsymbol {\xi} \right| ^ {3 k _ {1}} + 1\right)} _ {b _ {2} \left(\boldsymbol {g} _ {k} ^ {\mathrm {t}} \boldsymbol {\xi}\right)}. \tag {285} \\ \end{array}
$$

in (a), we use (281) and (229); in (b), we use (282); in (c), we use (181) and assumption (a.4). it is clear that  $b_{1}(s)$  and  $b_{2}(s)$  in (284) and (285) satisfy  $\mathbb{e}b_1^4 (z),\mathbb{e}b_2^4 (z) < \infty$  for  $z\sim \mathcal{n}(0,1)$ . choosing  $b(s) = \max \{b_1(s),b_2(s)\}$  then gives us the desired result.
